For a layer in a switch it shows that switch's children, the switches it sits in as rows above; for a bone of a switch's child (selected), the switches its counterparts are in and those its action bone action keys; with nothing that fits, the rows that fitted last. Each row names the switch and what it shows now ("Open (keyed)", "(from the rig)", "(the default)"). Tiles are thumbnails with names (src/editors/switch_thumbnails.cc: each child drawn small as it looks on this frame, whether the switch shows it or not, framed with its siblings so mouth shapes keep their size and place; drawn again when the document or frame changes, three a frame), the pick outlined in white. Hovering a tile previews it in the viewport after a moment (switch_preview_set: shown in place of the pick, never keyed, no undo step, renders and exports keep the pick; orange corners on the tile, unlike a pick's outline; Preview turns it off); a click picks and keys (Alt: the whole hold); dragging across a row's tiles scrubs, picking where it lets go (Esc cancels); over the shelf the arrows preview tile by tile, Enter picks, F2 or a double-click on a name renames. A tile's right-click menu: Duplicate, Duplicate with Keys, Rename, Set as Default (layer.switch_set default=1: frame 0's pick from any frame), Replace Keys With, Show in Layers Editor, Pin, Delete ("used by 12 keys"). The + tile adds an empty drawing (layer.switch_add_child), last, picked and keyed at this frame. The Switch menus in the Layers editor and the viewport show each child's thumbnail beside its name: the thumbnail popup at the mouse. Later: Set Label (with labels), keyboard shortcuts per tile.
- **Duplicate (stage 4, done).** layer.switch_duplicate(child=, keys=, start_from=SETUP|THIS_FRAME, action_bone_keys=). **Duplicate** copies a child without its timeline keys, keeping its action bone action keys and drivers; its bones stand in for the original's (bone swaps); the copy goes after the original with its name open for typing on the shelf, picked and keyed at this frame (on frame 0 only added); one undo step. **Duplicate with Keys** keeps the timeline keys too. The ViewportActionPanel has Start From (Setup; or This Frame: its own bones first take this pose as their rest through Apply Pose as Rest Pose, bending its drawings, then its drawings take how they look now as their setup, layer_strip_keys), Timeline Keys and Action Bone Keys. Its two-way links come along.
- **Linked by name (done).** A Mouth switch in each view of a Heads switch shows the same mouth whichever view is up (research: reports/Linked switches by name.md; Harmony's Sync Layer With is the nearest shipped tool, Character Animator's visemes by tag the nearest idea). Same-named switches at the same place inside a switch's drawings link by themselves (Heads > any view > Mouth; **Link Same-Named Switches** on the outer switch, on by default, the status bar saying so the first time); **Link Switches** (the Layers editor's right-click menu with two or more switches picked; layer.switch_link) links any, the active one's keys kept. A group has one set of keys, on its lead's Switch channel, holding entries of the group's list of names (Document::switch_links: ids, so a rename keeps them; files and Python see names): a pick in any member keys the group, with Auto Keying, Alt for the whole hold, Rig keys and hand keys beating action bone actions as before. Each member shows its drawing answering to the name: its name, or one of its **aliases** (the asset shelf's Aliases, layer.switch_aliases_set: the profile "Oh" also answering to O, a view's own naming). A member lacking the keyed name keeps showing the last name it had (the latest key before it that it has, then the default, then its own setup drawing), worked out frame by frame so scrubbing and renders agree; its timeline bar says "Special (not here: keeps Happy)" and its row in the Layers editor carries an orange warning naming what it lacks. No tool surveyed holds on purpose (Spine falls back to its default skin, Moho to a default, Unity can blank, Character Animator stops lip-sync at a turn); a view-only mouth is simply picked while that view shows and the other picked again when turning back, so there are no per-view override keys (Figma's override troubles). Renaming a drawing renames its partners and the group's name (Rename Here Only on the shelf, layer.rename_here, renames just one). A duplicated view's switch joins; a lead deleted hands the keys to another member; **Unlink** (the Switch menu; layer.switch_unlink) gives a member its own keys showing what it showed, nothing popping, and keeps it from linking again by name; a group left with one member ends. Linked switches carry a teal chain in the Layers editor (a click selects the partners), the shelf reads "Front > Mouth (linked: 2)", the timeline lists the group's keys while working in any member, and the Switch menu's **Link Report** shows names by switches: yes, alias, missing, or nearly ("happy.001", " copy", case: names must match exactly). Python: switch_linked, switch_link_names, switch_missing, switch_aliases, link_same_named; oanim.wm.switch_link_report. Later: one lip-sync track for the group, its dialog a grid of letters by views with gaps to fill with aliases.
- **Later.** Lip-sync through a mapping table (Rhubarb A-H and X, Preston Blair, Adobe's visemes; several phonemes to one child), importing Rhubarb and Moho's .dat, its keys in their own layer; Set switches for outfits and characters, children matched by label; frame-by-frame stays its own layer type, with Convert to Switch and back; an optional fade between children.
- **Making one, and Python.** The Layers editor's + menu has Switch, and Convert to Switch makes one of a group; layers moved into it are its children. Python: oanim.ops.layer.new_switch(), oanim.ops.layer.wrap_in_switch(), oanim.ops.layer.switch_set(layer=, child=name, "" for None, or "RIG"; hold="1" for the whole hold; without layer= the active layer, or the switch holding it), oanim.ops.layer.switch_next() and switch_previous(), oanim.ops.layer.switch_replace_keys(layer=, from=, child=), and on a layer switch_active (the child shown now, or None) and switch_default; its keys are the "switch" channel (layer.keyframes("switch")). A child a switch isn't showing keeps its own visibility: it is switched out (Layer::shown() as the viewport shows it, Layer::shown_in_render() as renders do), left out of drawing, picking, editing and exports, never hidden.

**Symmetrize layers.** The Layers editor's right-click menu has **Symmetrize + to -** and **Symmetrize - to +**: they make the other side of the active and picked layers, and of the layers in picked groups, mirrored across the document's vertical axis (x = 0); inside a bone layer, across the bone layer's own axis, the line bone Symmetrize uses. Partners pair by name, with the same left/right rule bones use (Ear_L and Ear_R, L arm and R arm, Left and Right; a trailing number kept). Each pair copies from whichever of the two sits on the side the command copies from (the middle of its drawing's box on the setup frame), whatever its name says, so either can be picked. A missing partner is made just above the source, in the same group, so a Clip to Below source and its copy clip to the same layer. An existing one is remade in place: it keeps its name, its place and its id, so masks naming it still do. A group named for a side goes as a whole: its layers flip their names where they have a side, keep their ids when they're remade, and are numbered when a name is taken ("Sleeve 1"). The copy's transform is the source's mirrored, and the layers inside it get their position's x and their rotation negated, so they move and turn the way the source's do. Drawings flip with their handles, widths, corners and detail points. Bone weights and Parent Bone move to the other side's bones (brow_L to brow_R). A bone layer inside a mirrored group has its bones mirrored across its own vertical axis, from the setup pose, each taking its other side's name (arm_L and arm_R swap) while keeping its id, so the weights, Parent Bone, custom shapes and constraints of the layers inside it stay on the mirrored bones; Limit Angle ranges flip, and its bones' keys (action bone actions' too) are left behind, as the drawings' are. Every link naming a layer mirrored with it names that layer's copy (references, masks, Draw Order, bones' custom shapes, Limit to Shape constraints, a deform's shape, a Boolean's targets); one naming a sided layer outside names its other side (a row of Eye_L becomes Eye_R; Head_X stays). A reference stays a reference when its original is mirrored with it (it shows the copy, what it moved mirrored too) or has another side to show (Ear_L's reference shows Ear_R); any other is copied as a plain layer showing what it showed. A Mirror modifier's centre goes across with the drawing. A picked drawing named for neither side is symmetrized in place, as point Symmetrize does with all its points (each needs a partner across the layer's axis, or it's skipped). It works on the setup frame only. The copies rest in the setup pose: no point, handle or transform keys, so animate them after. It's one undo step ("Symmetrize + to -"), and the status bar lists what it made, remade, symmetrized and skipped (with why: "not on the + side", "3 points on the right, 0 on the left"). Python: oanim.ops.layer.symmetrize(direction="POSITIVE_TO_NEGATIVE" or "NEGATIVE_TO_POSITIVE").

**Links stay relative.** Duplicate (Shift D in the Layers editor), Symmetrize, Copy and Paste and a switch's Duplicate keep the links inside what they copy pointing inside it: a reference, mask row, Draw Order, bone custom shape, Limit to Shape constraint, deform's shape or Boolean target naming a layer copied with it names the copy (across several picked layers too), so a duplicated rig is self-contained; one naming a layer left behind keeps naming that layer (pasted into another file, the layer of that name there, else nothing).

**Deselect All Layers.** In the Layers editor, **Alt A** (layer.select_none) or a click on its empty space deselects every layer, as Blender's Outliner does: the active layer stays active, for editing and keys, shown outlined but unpicked (Ctrl click on the only picked layer does the same), so the timeline's Only Selected shows nothing until a layer, points or bones are picked; a click on a layer, or making it active, picks it again.

**Copy and paste layers.** With the mouse over the Layers editor, **Ctrl C** copies the active layer and the picked ones, with everything in them, and **Ctrl V** pastes them above the active layer, in its group, into this file, another file or another running Open Animate (they travel on the system clipboard). They paste in their order, the one that was active when copied active again and the others picked; one undo step ("Paste 2 Layers"). Names already taken are numbered ("Ear_L 1"). Masks and Draw Order naming layers that came along name the pasted copies; ones naming layers left behind find this file's layer of the same name, and a mask row with none left goes. A shared material with the same name and look is reused, otherwise it is added (its name made unique). Pasted into a bone layer, Parent Bone and weights find its bones by name (a weight for a bone it doesn't have goes, so a point left with none is weighed as a new point is); pasted outside one, they let go. A layer moved into another bone layer (dragged, Move to Group) is bound the same way, by name; moved out of every bone layer, it lets go. A reference whose original wasn't copied pastes as a plain layer showing what it showed; drivers wholly inside the copy come along. Python: oanim.ops.layer.copy(), oanim.ops.layer.paste().

**Copy and paste shapes.** With the mouse over the viewport, **Ctrl C** copies what Shift D would of the drawings being edited (whole shapes, and pieces of line between selected points), and **Ctrl V** pastes them into the active drawing, on top and selected, into this file, another file or another running Open Animate; one undo step ("Paste Shapes"). A bone layer or a reference can't take them, and it says so. The Adjust Last Operation panel has **Keep Place** (on at first: they land exactly where they looked when copied, whatever this layer's place or what its bones do on this frame; off, the same numbers in this layer's own space) and **Keyframes** (off at first: they paste as they looked on the frame they were copied on, with no keys, and that pose is their setup, on every frame; on, their point, handle, width and curvature keys come on their own frames, on the mainline and on the action lines this layer is in, each key's change taking any difference in the two layers' turn and scale; keys of an action it isn't in are left out, and the report says which). Weights find this layer's bones by name, a point left with none weighed as a new point is; materials go as Paste Layers'; a point group this drawing has too takes the pasted points in, and one it hasn't isn't made. Shapes pasted over the Layers editor make a new layer with their own layer's settings; layers pasted over the viewport paste as layers. Python: oanim.ops.mesh.copy(), oanim.ops.mesh.paste(keep_place=, keyframes=).

**Grouping layers.** With the mouse over the Layers editor, **Ctrl G** (Group With Selection, also in its right-click menu) puts the active layer and the picked ones into a new group (a folder) where the active one was, in their order; a layer inside a picked group goes with that group. The new group becomes the active layer. One undo step; Python: oanim.ops.layer.group().

**Reference layers.** A reference layer is a live copy of another layer or group, in the same file or in another .oanim file. It shows the original's drawing, bones, action bones and animation, and updates as the original changes. Open Animate copies Moho's rules:

- **Layer channels follow the original** (location, rotation, scale, opacity, animation and all) until you edit or key that channel on the reference. The channel is then changed on the copy: teal in the Transform panel and the timeline, where right-clicking it offers Reset to Original (its keys go too) and Push to Original.
- **Settings that are not keyable stay local** to each reference: masks, blend mode, label colour, lock and similar. This is what makes advanced masking work. A hidden reference of an animated body can be the mask of shading, textures or outlines anywhere, so they are clipped to the body's exact animated shape with no extra animation.
- **Referencing a group** references its whole tree; every child is itself a reference.
- **Making one:** **Alt D** in the Layers editor (Blender's Duplicate Linked; Shift D is an ordinary copy), the Layers editor's Ref button or right-click › Reference Layer: Duplicate as Reference, a reference of the active layer just above it.
- **A reference of a reference links to the original** (references never chain): Duplicate as Reference on a reference, or on a referenced group, makes a plain reference of the original layer (inside a group, of each original layer), without the copy's own changes. Files with a chain, made before this, open with it linked to the original.
- **Its own changes, each kept apart** (overrides, as Blender's library overrides, Unity's prefab overrides and Figma's instances keep theirs), found by the original's permanent point and shape ids so they stay on the right ones as the original changes. Everything not changed follows the original.
  - **Looks:** per shape and per setting (fill colour, line colour, line width, brush, fill and outline on), edited in the Shape tab's Shape panel as on any layer; a colour, line width or brush changed on a copy shows a revert button beside it in the Shape panel, and with its points locked the shape's Position is greyed out (point editing is locked); a shape of the original can be hidden on the reference only (from Python, layer.reference_hide_shape; listed under Changes, to reset); an edge can be hidden or shown on the reference only, with the Edge panel as on any layer, whether its points are locked or not (kept by the ids of the points at either end; Reset Looks brings back the original's); the Reference tab's **Recolor** has just Reset All Shape Styles and Push All Shape Styles (every colour, line width, brush and fill or outline changed on the copy, at once; hidden shapes stay hidden). Colour swaps (one colour of the original recoloured on every shape using it, the ones added later too) can still be made from Python (layer.reference_swap_color) and are listed under Changes.
  - **Points: Locked / Editable** (the Reference tab, or the lock in the viewport header beside Points / Edges / Strokes; locked by default, so points don't move by accident): moving a reference's points keeps offsets from the original's points, keyed like any edit; the offsets' keys add to the original's motion. **Follow Original's Animation** (under More, on by default); off, only the original's setup shape shows, with the reference's own keys. Handles turned or dragged are kept as offsets too (found by their point and the neighbour each points toward), added to the original's handles the same way. Widths and curve types stay the original's, and points can't be added or deleted (a message says so; Break Reference to change the drawing).
  - **Pose: Same as Original / Own Bones** (Same as Original by default): the reference shows the original's final shape, posed by its bones, action bones and dynamics, wherever it sits (even outside the bone layer, as exact masks need); the top reference's own transform takes the place of the original's (and follows it until taken over), and everything inside a referenced group follows the original. With Own Bones, it binds to its own bone layer, starting with the original's weights by bone name (or automatic ones); its weights are its own, and Weight Paint changes them without touching the original's. Its Parent Bone is greyed out until it has Own Bones (posed Same as Original, it moves with the original's bones, so a parent bone set earlier waits).
  - **The Reference tab** (on references) holds everything about the link: "Copy of <original>" with Go to Original and how many changes it has, then a dropdown each: Pose, Points and Recolor (open), **Changes on This Copy** (folded to start with: grouped as Colors, Shapes, Points, Layer Animation and Bone Weights, each opening to its items, clicking one picks it in the viewport, with Reset and Push on each item and group) and More (Follow Original's Animation, Remove Unused Changes); Break Reference apart, with a warning. One vocabulary everywhere: **Reset** (back to the original) and **Push** (written into the original, so it and every copy get it; hiding and own weights stay the copy's).
  - **Changes show where they are:** a value the copy changed shows teal where it's edited (the Shape panel's settings, Transform, Opacity, Mean Position, the timeline's rows), as Blender shows overridden properties, and right-clicking it offers Reset to Original and Push to Original. The Layers editor marks a copy with changes with a teal dot, and while a copy is selected its original's row is outlined (or the folded group holding it). Something not allowed on a copy (deleting or adding its points, moving them while locked, changing widths or curve types) is undone, and a warning shows in the status bar's reports banner, as in Blender (also when a copy's selected points stay put while other layers' points move, as when editing from a bone layer); the same goes for the viewport's and rigging's other refusals (drawing away from the setup frame, changing the rig away from it, a bone hanging from its own child).
- **References to another file** show a warning when the original has changed. Updating offers Moho's options: add new layers, remove missing layers, replace mismatched bones, replace mismatched drawings.
- **Break reference** turns it into an ordinary layer that keeps what it shows, its offsets added to its points' keys so its animation stays as it was; posed Same as Original, the original's modifiers it showed through (a Resample, Mirror, Bevel, deforms; its bones as its own Armature) become its own, the original's Booleans (which never showed on it) stay behind, and its own other modifiers, which didn't show, are kept turned off. A reference whose original is deleted does the same.
- **Better than Moho:** each channel stores its override flag explicitly, so a reference never silently gains keys or loses its link. Moho users report both problems ([forum thread](https://lostmarble.net/forum/viewtopic.php?t=37872)).

Sources on Moho's behaviour: the scripting API (`DuplicateLayer` by reference, `UpdateReferencedLayer`, `MohoLayerRefSyncOptions`) and [this forum thread](https://lostmarble.net/forum/viewtopic.php?t=34787).

**Tools.** A tool is an object with mouse, key and tablet callbacks, an overlay draw callback, an options bar, saved preferences, and checks that hide or grey it out by context. That maps directly onto our Python tool class.

## Feature spec

Version 1 covers 67 of the 98 features below: enough to draw, rig with action bones, animate on a timeline and export frames. The rest are tiered Later or Maybe. Change any tier from its dropdown. "Community" means a feature that Moho users built as add-on scripts, so it becomes native here. Speed is a requirement throughout, not a feature: the viewport should play back at the document frame rate.

**Viewport and navigation**

| Feature | From | Tier |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Camera view with pan, zoom and rotate | Both | v1 |
| An infinite canvas and a Camera object: picked (its Layers row, or its frame's border), the viewport looks through it and panning and zooming move and zoom it; keyed Position, Zoom (at a steady rate) and Roll; a Passepartout darkens the canvas outside it; renders show its frame (see below) | Blender | v1 |
| World/Camera view toggle: a free 3D view outside the camera to see every layer in depth (see below) | Blender | v1 |
| Onion skins: a toggle beside the timeline's record button, with a popover beside it as auto-keying's. The drawings being edited (and every drawing inside a picked bone layer or group; All Layers for every drawing) show as they are on other frames, as wireframes of what they render (no fills, construction curves or guides), under the edited layer's wires and never rendered: Keyframes steps by the keys of those layers and of the bone layers and groups moving them (a key on the frame shown doesn't count), Frames by frames, every Step frames (2 for animating on twos); Before and After say how many each side, each in its own colour (red before, green after by default), fainter further away with Fade; ghosts stay in the animation's range (frame 0 is the setup, never a ghost). The ghosted frames are tinted faintly in the timeline. Hidden while playing and in Media View. Saved with the project. Python: document.onion_skin, onion_mode (KEYFRAMES, FRAMES), onion_before, onion_after, onion_step, onion_fade, onion_all_layers; oanim.wm.onion_frames(), onion_wires(), onion_colors(), set_onion_colors(before=, after=) | Both | v1 |
| Grid, guides and snapping | Both | v1 |
| Split any area into more editors, and join them back | Blender | v1 |
| Left toolbar with Box Select (default) and Cursor tools | Blender | v1 |
| Separate 2D cursor (Camera view) and 3D cursor (World view) | Blender, Open Animate | v1 |
| Header dropdowns: transform orientation, pivot point, proportional editing | Blender | v1 |
| Snapping dropdown in the header: snap to points, curves, grid or bones | Blender | v1 |

**World/Camera view toggle.** A toggle button in the viewport header switches the view between Camera and World; Numpad 0 does the same, as in Blender.

- **Camera:** you see exactly what renders, framed by the camera. The camera tools (track, zoom, roll, pan/tilt) move the scene camera.
- **World:** a free view you can orbit, pan and zoom around the whole scene. Layers show at their real depth, the camera appears as an outlined frame you can select and move, and imported meshes are visible from any angle.
- Each viewport remembers its own World position, so toggling back returns you to where you were. In a split layout, one viewport can stay in Camera while another is in World.

**The camera.** The canvas is infinite: the background goes on past the camera's frame. The camera is pinned at the top of the Layers editor; it isn't a layer, so it can't be moved in the list, hidden or locked. At the origin, zoom 1 and unrolled, its frame is where the page always was (2 units tall), so files from before cameras open unchanged.

- Picking it (its Layers row, or a click on its frame's border where nothing else is) makes the viewport look through it, as Blender's Lock Camera to View: the view fits the frame, drawn orange, and panning and zooming move and zoom the camera, one undo step per gesture. With it not picked the view moves freely (the wheel zooms at the mouse, middle drag pans, Ctrl + middle drag zooms about the middle of the view, as in Blender: up zooms in, down out), and Home frames the camera.
- The Passepartout (0 to 1) darkens the canvas outside the frame.
- While it is picked, Properties has a Camera tab: Position, Zoom and Roll, each with its key button, and Passepartout. Its keys show in the timeline under Camera, and under Selected while it is picked. The zoom's keys hold log2 of the zoom, so between keys it changes at a steady rate (from 1 to 4 it passes 2 halfway).
- Renders show exactly the frame. Render > Render Frame shows it in the Render Result window, with Save As...; right-clicking the image offers Copy Image (also Ctrl C over the window: to the clipboard as a PNG, keeping transparency for apps that read it, and as a bitmap for the rest) and Save As... (Python: oanim.wm.clipboard_set_image(rgba, width, height) puts any image there the same way). Python: oanim.data.camera (position, zoom, roll, passepartout, selected, keyframes(), to_frame()).
- File > Export: SVG (Current Frame)..., PDF (Current Frame)... and PDF (Frame Range, a Page a Frame)... write frames through the camera as vectors, at the document's size, each looking as Render Frame's picture does. One walk of the frame (src/io/vector_export.cc) makes a tree both files are written from: fills as their Bezier outlines; each line as one filled outline of what the renderer draws for it (its width at every point, joins and ends; the stroke's triangles merged by Clipper2), ended where End Lines at Edge ends it; a brushed line as a picture of its stamps, laid out as the renderer lays them and stamped on the CPU at twice the size; masks as luminance masks painted as the cards paint theirs (Start With, Add and Subtract, Invert, Union, Difference and Intersection, Grow and Shrink, Extend, Fills or Strokes Only, Inside the Stroke, Clip to Below; a card that masks only fills or only lines on just those); faded groups and see-through layers as groups with an opacity; media pictures shown in renders as pictures, greyed and tinted as drawn. The PDF (src/io/pdf_export.cc, written directly, streams compressed with miniz) has a page a frame (a pixel three quarters of a point) and every layer as a PDF layer (optional content) named and nested as in the Layers editor, so Acrobat or Illustrator can switch layers off and on; hidden layers and guides are in it too, switched off (a hidden mask layer still masks: masks are made apart from the layers shown); masks are soft masks, faded groups transparency groups; other frames are worked out on a copy of the document. Drawn by Chrome (SVG) and Windows' PDF renderer, the files match the app's PNG render but for anti-aliasing along edges: every render_check mask and fill case, brushed lines, a media picture, the Clay rigs and Rainbow Dash, no pixel more than 128 of 255 apart and at most 0.1% more than 64. Rainbow Dash: SVG 3 MB in 0.6 s; PDF 11 MB in 10 s (its 3,156 layers, 1,184 of them hidden). Python: oanim.ops.export.svg(filepath=..., width=, height=, transparent=), oanim.ops.export.pdf(filepath=..., frames="CURRENT" or "RANGE", frame_start=, frame_end=, width=, height=, transparent=).

**Number fields.** Every number field (Properties, headers, the ViewportActionPanel, Brush Settings, the timeline) works as Blender's: dragging it stays within its soft limits (from a value already past them it can come back but goes no further out); typing a value is held only to its hard limits, which are usually unlimited. A size or count can't be typed below its least, and a 0-1 fraction or a 0-100% amount can't be typed past its top; a property's own range (opacity's 0-1, shear's 89 degrees) is its hard limit, with an optional soft range for dragging. Sculpt's Strength is a factor bar filled across 0-1, dragged within it, typed as high as wanted, as Blender's (past 1, Grab and Push move further than the mouse, Pinch and Twist work harder, never past the centre, and Smooth takes more passes).

**The number field.** One widget (`src/editors/number_field.cc`) draws them all, ported from Blender's number buttons. Its label is on the left and its value on the right (the value alone is centred); a label without room loses its end. It has no arrows. A 0-1 value (opacity, weight, a strength, an option ranged 0-1) is a factor: a bar filled to where the value sits in its soft range. Dragging across, after a 3 pixel threshold, hides the cursor and reads only the mouse's motion (no screen edge to run into); letting go shows it again where it was pressed. A factor's full width is its whole soft range. Shift drags ten times finer, Ctrl snaps (by 0.1 under a range of about 2, else by 1; integers by 10), and Esc or a right click puts the value back. A click without dragging types into it. A typed sum (2*30+1, 1/3, parentheses, ^) is worked out, and Tab moves to the next field. Over a field, Ctrl+wheel steps it, minus negates it, Ctrl+C and Ctrl+V copy and paste its value, and Backspace resets it to its default where it has one. Dragging up or down first picks the fields passed over (X Y Z of a location), and then dragging across changes them all by the same amount. A value pen pressure can scale has a pen toggle joined to its right end: Draw's Width, Sculpt's Radius and Strength, and Weight Paint's Weight, Radius and Strength.

**Viewport toolbar.** A Blender-style toolbar runs down the left side of the viewport, in boxes always in this order, each its own panel: General (Select, Select Layer, Transform Layer, Cursor, Annotate), Drawing (Transform Points and Sculpt, then under a bar the frame-0 Draw, Shape and Split Curve), Rig (Transform Bones, then under a bar the frame-0 Weight Select and Weight Paint) and Text (Edit Text); hovering a box's edge names it. Select shows where there is something to pick: a drawing's points, a bone layer's bones, a group's drawings; not on a text or Media layer, which start with Transform Layer instead. Transform Layer (any layer, as Moho's): a box round what the active layer shows, in its own space (turning and scaling with it): drag inside to move the layer, the band round it (or the arcs at its corners) to turn it about its pivot (Shift: 45° steps), a handle to scale it, the opposite one staying put (Shift keeps proportions, Alt scales from the pivot), an edge between handles to shear it (its Shear X or Y: its Rotation stays, its Scale and Location keep the opposite edge still; Alt about the pivot, Shift in 15° steps); the pivot is the layer's origin until dragged (Ctrl snaps it to the box's corners, sides' middles and middle; a double-click puts it back), isn't saved, and G, R and S turn and scale about it too; Esc or right-click cancels; it sets the layer's Location, Rotation, Scale and Shear, keyed as any change (undo steps Move, Rotate, Scale and Shear Layer). While it is the tool, G, R and S move, turn and scale the active layer itself whatever it holds, and its points aren't shown (they stay selected); greyed out on a locked layer. With Show Grid Lines (in Tool Settings, on at first) the viewport shows the active layer's own grid: lines at even steps of its own units through everything that places it (its transform, its groups', a bone it follows), so a layer squashed across has tall thin cells and a turned one a turned grid; one step both ways (from its average scale, in powers of 2, 24 to 48 pixels apart), so a squash shows; every 4th line stronger, and its own X axis (red) and Y axis (green) through its origin; it follows the box while it is dragged and hides with the overlays. Python: oanim.ops.wm.tool_transform_layer(), wm.tool_edit_text(), oanim.wm.layer_grid_lines(on). What shows goes by the active layer, as Moho's tools and Blender's modes do: a vector layer has General and Drawing, and inside a bone layer the Rig box's weight tools too; a bone layer has General, Drawing's Transform Points and Sculpt (which move what is drawn: making artwork isn't done there) and Rig's Transform Bones; a group has General and Drawing's Transform Points and Sculpt; a Media layer, General; a text layer, General and Text. A tool that can never work for the active layer doesn't show; one you can make work is greyed out, its tooltip and a click on it saying how: frame-0 tools on other frames ("press Tab"), and Transform Points and Sculpt on a bone layer or group with no drawings to edit ("turn on Editable in Bone Group" or Edit Descendants). Each kind of active layer (drawing, bone layer, group, Media, text, none) keeps the tool last used with it, as Blender's modes do: making that kind active again picks it again (the first time, and when it can't show: Select, else Transform Layer, else Select Layer). Taller than the viewport, the toolbar goes to two columns.

**Box gizmos.** Transform Points, Transform Bones and Transform Layer share one box, after Affinity's, Animate's and Harmony's: a line in the mode's colour over a dark edge (Transform blue, Distort orange, Warp purple), white square handles that fill with the colour under the mouse and can be grabbed from about twice their drawn size, short arcs just outside the corners where a drag turns it, and the pivot a ring with a crosshair. While dragging, the box goes with what it moves (turning, stretching and slanting), a grey dot marks where the pivot was, and a readout by the cursor gives the move, the angle, the scale (120% × 100%), the shear or the mode. A small selection still gets a box at least 48 pixels across, and a handle keeps the spot it was grabbed by. A double-click on the pivot puts it back in the middle. Dragging an edge between two handles shears: the top or bottom slides sideways, a side up and down, about the opposite edge (Alt: the pivot; Shift: steps of 15°); on points Ctrl on a side handle does the same. Ctrl-dragging a corner distorts it on its own. Transform Points and Transform Bones have three modes, switched in the header (Transform, Distort, Warp, and Warp's grid) or from a right-click on the box (which also has Reset Pivot and Box): Transform moves, turns, scales and shears; Distort drags each corner on its own, a side's middle moving its two corners, and inside still moves; Warp lays a grid over the selection (3 cells each way at first, 1 to 8) whose nodes, or any place inside, can be dragged, bending everything inside smoothly through them (Catmull-Rom curves through the nodes, so each node is on the bent surface and an even grid changes nothing; points with their handles, bones by their bases and tips). A Distort or Warp grid stays bent between drags, each drag one undo step from before the first, until the selection, the frame, the mode or anything else in the document changes. A stem out of the top of the box with a square knob on its end turns the box's own axes, not what is in it (Shift: 15° steps; a double-click squares it again), so scaling, shearing, Distort and Warp then work along them; a turn made by rotating turns the box with it (as Illustrator's and Affinity's do) until something else changes the document (an undo squares it again), and a new selection squares it. The header's typed fields (Position, Scale, Angle, and Shear: degrees, x leaning sideways and y up and down, about the pivot) show what a drag is doing while it goes. Shears, Distort and Warp end as one step of mesh.cage_transform or armature.cage_transform (lo_x, lo_y, hi_x, hi_y, cells, nodes: the grid's corners where they went, row by row from the bottom; box_angle; name), as does a scale of bones along a turned box's axes. Python: oanim.wm.gizmo_mode(mode, cells).

| Tool | What it does | Keys and options |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Box Select (default; T with the mouse over the viewport switches back to it from any tool) | Drag a rectangle to select, exactly as in Blender | Drag for a new selection, Shift-drag to add, Ctrl-drag to subtract; a click picks the nearest point or bone part, and clicking again in the same spot (the mouse not moved) picks the next one under it (round to the nearest again), so points or bones on top of each other can each be picked, as Select Layer does with layers; Shift-click does the same, adding or taking out the next one; header buttons for set, extend, subtract, invert and intersect; A selects all, Alt+A deselects |
| Select Layer | Click the drawing to select its layer: the topmost shown there (Draw Order included), and only where it really shows (its fills and lines, where its masks let it). Media layers, hidden layers, hidden guides and layers outside Local View are never picked; clicking a bone picks its bone layer. Clicking again in the same spot picks the one below (round to the top again). The layer that would be picked is outlined and named as you hover (a box round its drawing as it shows: moved by its transform, bones and modifiers, curves and line widths included) | Shift-click adds or takes out, Ctrl-click takes out. Pick: Layer / Group in Tool Settings (Group takes the outermost group, as Photoshop's Auto-Select). **Alt-click** picks from any tool, **Alt-Shift-click** adds (the Shape tool's Alt-drag, a handle's Alt-drag and the transform boxes' Alt-drags keep their meaning; only a click picks, on the boxes and their pivots too). Python: oanim.ops.layer.select_at(x=, y=, mode=SET/TOGGLE/REMOVE, group=, layer=). **=** (anywhere) or **Alt right-click** (in the viewport) goes up the tree: the group holding the active layer becomes the active layer alone (Select Hair_X from Hair_Details_X), one undo step each; Python: oanim.ops.layer.select_parent() |
| Cursor | Click or drag to place the cursor | Shift+right-click places the cursor from any tool, as in Blender |
| Draw | Draw freehand strokes onto the active vector layer, like Blender's Grease Pencil Draw tool; tablet pressure sets line width. Each stroke becomes an editable curve, simplified to a few points and welded to nearby points it touches | Ctrl-drag erases, as in Blender; F resizes the brush and Shift+F changes strength; header options for radius, pressure on or off, stabiliser, auto-close and fill, and the style to draw with |
| Split Curve | Click on a line to add a point there without changing the curve's shape. Works on vector layers, and across all selected layers with multi-layer editing | K switches to this tool from any tool. Shift-click adds the point at the exact middle of the segment; Ctrl-click deletes a point while keeping the curve's shape as closely as possible. Based on the community hs\_splitsegment script |
| Knife | Like Blender's knife: click points to draw a cut path across shapes. Where the cut crosses a line a point is added, and inside a shape the cut becomes a new internal segment welded at both ends. A filled shape the cut crosses is split into separate shapes that keep its style | Ctrl+K from any tool. Click adds a cut point, double-click closes the cut, Enter or Space confirms, Esc cancels, right-click starts a new cut, Ctrl+Z removes the last cut point. Hold Shift to snap to segment midpoints, Ctrl to ignore snapping; X or Y locks the axis, A toggles angle snapping, C toggles cutting through every overlapping shape instead of only the top one |
| Annotate | Notes and drawing guides over the drawing, on every frame and any layer (or none); never part of it. One tool bar button whose flyout (right-click it) holds Annotate, Annotate Line, Annotate Eraser, Guide Line, Guide Perspective and Guide Grid; the button shows the one last picked. See **Annotations and drawing guides** below | Annotate: drag to draw. Annotate Line: drag a straight line, with Start and End styles (None, Arrow, Open Arrow, Segment, Dot) in the header. Annotate Eraser: drag to rub out what's under the circle (its Radius in the header). The guide tools: drag on empty canvas to make a guide, drag a handle to change it, drag its line to move it all; Ctrl while dragging snaps; a Guide Line also turns from beside the picked one, copies on a double-click and deletes on a right-click or Alt-click; X or Delete deletes the guide line under the mouse or being dragged, else the picked guide |

**Annotations and drawing guides.** Notes and visual aids drawn over the drawing, never part of it: they aren't layers (the Layers editor doesn't list them), are never rendered, aren't masked or moved by bones, look the same on every frame, and the drawing tools never snap to them or change because of them. They live in document space, so they stay on the drawing as the view pans and zooms, and are drawn over the drawing, under the points, handles and bones. Overlays > Annotations shows or hides the annotations, Overlays > Guides the drawing guides along with the guide layers, and Shift Z (the overlays off) hides both. Saved with the file; every change is an undo step.

- **Annotations**, as Blender's: strokes on annotation layers, each layer with a name, a colour, a thickness (screen pixels, so a note reads the same at any zoom) and Hide and Lock. The Annotate tools' header shows the active layer's colour and a dropdown of the layers (pick one, add one, rename it by double-clicking, hide, lock, delete), then Thickness. Annotate draws freehand; Ctrl-drag erases, as the Draw tool's Ctrl does. Annotate Line draws a straight line; its Start and End styles draw an arrowhead (Arrow, Open Arrow), a bar (Segment) or a dot at either end. Annotate Eraser rubs out the parts of strokes under its circle on the shown, unlocked layers, splitting a stroke where the circle crosses it. Drawing with no annotation layer makes one ("Note").
- **Drawing guides**, as Toon Boom's drawing guides and Krita's assistants, but only to look at: **Line**, a measure between its two ends with a tick across each, as Moho's sz_draw_guides script draws them (the tool button has the script's icon); **Perspective**, one, two or three vanishing points (Points in the header) each with a fan of lines (Lines in the header: how many go round each point), and the horizon through the first two (level through a lone one); **Grid**, square cells from its origin handle, the second handle setting the cell's size and turn. All guides share one colour, the header's Hue at full strength, and its Opacity; Show guides turns them all off, and with it off the guide tools have nothing to touch. A Line is 2 pixels wide, 4 when picked. Perspectives and Grids are thin and cover the whole view (the picked one stronger, the one under the mouse a little stronger); a guide tool shows every guide even with Overlays > Guides off, as they are what it works on. With a guide tool, every shown, unlocked Perspective and Grid shows its handles: drag one to change the guide, drag the guide's line to move all of it, click a guide to pick it. A Line shows no handles: drag an end to change it, drag its line to move it; on the picked Line, drag past an end to turn that end round the other, or within 40 pixels alongside it to turn it round its middle, both keeping its length, and a click there drops the pick. Double-click a Line and drag to pull a copy away. Right-click or Alt-click a Line, or round the picked one, to delete it; X or Delete does too, over a Line or while dragging it; so does dragging one end onto the other. Dragging on empty canvas makes a new guide of the tool's kind (Line: from one handle to the other; Perspective: from the first vanishing point to the second, the third below their middle; Grid: from the origin to the cell's corner) and picks it. **Ctrl** while dragging snaps: a Line keeps to steps of 45 degrees or its old angle (moving it, to the axes) and takes no notice of points or other guides; a Perspective's or Grid's handle goes to a point of the drawing or another guide's handle or line, else turns in steps of 15 degrees. The header has Show guides, Hue and Opacity; the picked Line's Length; Mirror H and Mirror V (across the document's centre); Delete All; and the picked guide's Points and Lines (perspective), Hide, Lock and Delete (X or Delete over the viewport). New guides of the tool's kind use the header's Points and Lines.
- Python: oanim.data.annotations (layers: name, color, thickness, hidden, locked, strokes: points, start and end styles), oanim.data.active_annotation, oanim.data.guides (type LINE, PERSPECTIVE or GRID, points, lines, hidden, locked), oanim.data.active_guide; the overlays as "annotations" and "guides" in oanim.wm.overlays() / set_overlay(); oanim.ops.annotation.draw(points=, start=, end=), annotation.erase(x=, y=, radius=), annotation.layer_add / layer_remove / layer_set; oanim.ops.guide.add(type=, points=), guide.set(index=, ...), guide.move_point(index=, point=, x=, y=, snap=), guide.delete(index=); oanim.ops.wm.tool_annotate(mode=DRAW, LINE, ERASER, GUIDE_LINE, GUIDE_PERSPECTIVE or GUIDE_GRID).

**Selection keys, as in Blender Grease Pencil edit mode.**

- **1, 2, 3** switch the selection mode: Points, Edges (clicking a line selects the segment between two points) or Strokes (clicking a line selects the whole curve). In Points mode only points are picked: clicking a line between them selects nothing. With edges selected, the Vector tab's Selected Edges panel hides or shows their outline, as Moho's Hide Edge does. Clicks pick lines where they show (posed by bones, and a reference matching its original where that is). Curve handles show only in Points mode: Edges and Strokes modes pick lines, so their handles are hidden.
- **L** selects the whole shape under the mouse; **Shift+L** deselects it.
- **Ctrl L** opens Link/Transfer Data, as Blender's. With points: **Link Materials**, the active shape's material (the shape last clicked; with a look of its own, that look) given to every shape with at least one point selected, on every layer being edited (select the shapes, then click the one to copy from last). On a bone layer: **Copy Constraints**, the other selected bones' constraints replaced by the active bone's (Blender's Copy Constraints to Selected Bones; theirs, and the drivers on them, are deleted first, so each ends with the active bone's stack; one aimed at the bone it would go on is left out), and **Copy Constraints with Drivers**: the same, with each copy's settings driven by the original's (Enabled, Influence and those its type uses: the angles, Amount, Factor and Delay, Chain and Max Stretch, and every Dynamics setting and spring), so changing the original changes every copy; the bone a constraint aims at stays as copied. Python: oanim.ops.material.link_selected(), armature.constraints_copy(drivers=), layer.set_active_shape(index). Always, under a line: **Copy Masks**, the active layer's mask cards and Clip to Below given to the other layers picked in the Layers editor, replacing theirs (a copied step naming the layer it lands on leaves that layer out, so nothing masks itself, and a step left naming none goes); one undo step; Python: oanim.ops.layer.masks_copy().
- The **Points, Edges and Strokes** buttons in the header show only when there are points to pick.
- **Shift+Z** over the viewport shows or hides its overlays (bones, wires, points, handles), as the header's Overlays button does, and with them every gizmo: the Transform boxes (nothing to grab; Transform Points and Bones' clicks select), Surface Smooth's ghost, Edit Text's box and wrap handles, Select Layer's outline, Move and Rotate's dashed line and proportional editing's circle. What a tool can't be used without stays: brush circles, a stroke being drawn, a box being dragged, the text caret, Weight Paint's colours, the guides while a guide tool is picked. A selected camera still shows its frame.

**Two cursors.** Camera view has a 2D cursor on the canvas and World view has a 3D cursor in the scene. They are separate, and each keeps its own position. New layers, bones and imported meshes are placed at the active view's cursor, and the cursor can be the pivot point. **Shift S** opens Blender's Snap pie round the mouse, drawn as Blender's (its items 100 pixels out at interface scale 1 and a unit and a half tall, the left ones ending there and the right ones starting there, its dark items with the lit one grey, a ring in the middle with a quarter of it blue the way the mouse points, each item's pointer or cursor icon and its number), its items where the numpad's keys are: Cursor to World Origin (1), Cursor to Selected (2), Cursor to Active (3), Cursor to Grid (4), Selection to Grid (6), Selection to Cursor (Keep Offset) (7), Selection to Cursor (8) and Selection to Active (9). Pointing the mouse an item's way lights it; a click or its number (top row or keypad) picks it, and so does letting go of S, as Blender's: when the mouse went out of the middle while S was held, however quickly, or S was held a while (a quick tap leaves the pie open to click); Esc, right-click or a click in the middle closes it. The selection is the picked bones (a bone layer), the active Media layer, or the selected points, where they show (bones and parents counted); active is the active bone's base, the active shape's middle (the shape last clicked) or the Media layer's centre. Points go each onto the target (Selection to Grid: each onto its nearest grid point; Keep Offset: the selection's middle goes there and the shape comes whole), with mirror editing and Keep Poses as a move; bones move together, by their middle, and while posing with Move Only Bones and Move Without Children as G has them (kept for later moves until the frame or action changes; in the Adjust Last Operation panel to change, and only_bones= and without_children= from Python). The cursor moves have no undo step (the cursor is a tool setting, as Blender's); the selection moves are one each. Python: oanim.ops.view.snap_cursor_to_center / _selected / _active / _grid, snap_selected_to_grid / _cursor(use_offset=) / _active; oanim.wm.cursor(), set_cursor((x, y)).

**Move, rotate, scale.** G moves, R rotates and S scales from any tool, as in Blender. Angles are plain numbers, as in Moho: R counts whole turns while the mouse circles the pivot, and layer rotation and bone angles key any amount, so one key at 720° spins twice. On points, S and R work about the middle of the selection's bounds (so S X -1 flips them in place, as Mirror Local does). For bones, S on frame 0 resizes them (their joints spread about the pivot, so bones get longer; scale stays 1). Posing, S scales each bone along its own axes: S X its thickness, S Y its length, and S S squashes and stretches (longer and thinner, keeping its area). Bone transforms take X / Y and typed numbers too. While moving, turning or scaling anything, Z keeps to the Y axis as Y does (up and down only), for hands used to Blender's vertical axis. Holding Shift during G, R or S (and Alt S) makes the mouse a tenth as sensitive, as Blender's precision: from where it was when Shift went down, so nothing jumps as it is pressed or let go (a Shift still held from the key that started the move, Shift D or Shift E, counts only once let go and pressed again, as in Blender, so a duplicate follows the mouse at once). **Slide (G G):** G again while moving points slides them along their curves, as Blender's Vertex Slide: each goes toward the neighbour the mouse heads for, by a fraction of the way there (a typed number from -1 to 1, negative toward the point before; Ctrl snaps to tenths), and the segments either side are refit to the line as it was, so the curve keeps its shape (exactly while a segment stays within one old segment); an open curve's end only slides inward, and G once more goes back to moving. One undo step (Slide), adjustable in the ViewportActionPanel; Python: oanim.ops.mesh.slide(factor=, keep_poses=).

- Then press **X, Y or Z** to lock to that axis; press it again to switch to the other orientation. **Shift+X, Y or Z** locks to the other two axes.
- Type a number for an exact value, as Blender's numeric input: once anything is typed the mouse stops moving it. Move has an X and a Y field (Tab goes to the other; along X or Y there's one), Scale one for every way (Tab to give Y its own), Rotate degrees. Minus flips the sign (before or after the digits), Backspace takes back a character and Ctrl Backspace the field; the header shows the field being typed as [1.5|]. Points, layers, bones and media all take it. Hold Ctrl to snap, Enter or click to confirm, Esc or right-click to cancel.
- **Alt S** with points selected scales the line's width at them (Blender Grease Pencil's Shrink/Fatten): away from their middle fattens, towards it thins, each point's radius scaled by the same factor; a typed number sets it, Ctrl snaps to tenths, Esc puts them back. A point's radius goes up to 10 times its shape's line width (Mean Radius in the Point panel too). On a Media layer Alt S is still Clear Scale, on a bone layer the bones'.
- Points and bones lie flat on their layer, so Z applies to things with depth: layers, the camera, the 3D cursor and meshes.

**Tool Settings.** Under the viewport header, as Blender's, a strip holds the settings of the tool in hand and nothing else, just wide enough for them, with the drawing showing beside it. A tool with nothing to set shows no strip, and its row stays clear, so switching tools moves nothing below it. The header above keeps what every tool shares: the menus, Points / Edges / Strokes, the reference lock and Detail on the left, mirror, snapping and proportional editing in the middle (as Blender's, staying put as the active layer changes), overlays and shading on the right. Where this spec speaks of a tool's header, it means this strip. The strip takes the clicks and the wheel over it, and when it doesn't fit the wheel scrolls it along. View > Tool Settings hides it (kept in the preferences). What the mouse does with a tool (Click: select | Shift: add) shows at the left of the status bar, as Blender's.

**Header dropdowns.** The viewport header carries Blender's three transform controls.

- **Transform orientation:** Global, Local (the layer's own axes), Parent (the parent bone), View or Cursor. Comma opens it as a pie menu.
- **Pivot point:** Bounding box centre, Median point, Individual origins, Active element or Cursor. Period opens it as a pie menu.
- **Proportional editing:** points only; the toggle and its dropdown show only while the edited layers have points. O toggles it. The dropdown, as Blender's panel, picks the falloff (Smooth, Sphere, Root, Inverse square, Sharp, Linear, Constant, Random; Shift+O cycles), Connected only (Alt+O: measured along the curves, so only points on a curve with selected points move) and the Proportional Size (0.2 to start). A pulled point's handles each come by their own distance, as Blender's take each Bezier handle as a point of its own: one nearer the selection moves further than its point, one beyond it less, so the curve bends into the falloff (with Connected only, measured along the curve from its point or the neighbour on its side). Scroll the mouse wheel (or Page Up / Page Down) during G, R or S to resize the influence circle drawn about what moves. Blender's Projected from View is left out: a drawing is already flat. This replaces Moho's Magnet tool.

**Drawing and vector editing**

| Feature | From | Tier |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Pen, in the Draw tool (right-click it to pick Draw or Pen): click to add points, auto-weld to existing points | Moho | v1 |
| Freehand and brush with tablet pressure setting line width | Moho | v1 |
| Shape tool: box and circle, picked from a toolbar flyout (right-click the tool); Shift keeps sides even, Alt draws from the centre | Both | v1 |
| Add menu (Shift A, as Blender's, on a vector layer; on a bone layer Shift A adds a bone): Point, Line, Square or Circle at the 2D cursor, a quarter of the frame across, in the Draw tool's look (Square and Circle filled when the tool fills); the ViewportActionPanel changes its place and size. Python: mesh.primitive_point_add (x, y), mesh.primitive_line_add (x, y, length, angle), mesh.primitive_box_add / primitive_circle_add | Blender | v1 |
| Annotate tool: notes (freehand, lines with arrowheads, an eraser) and drawing guides (line, perspective, grid) over the drawing, never rendered, on every frame; Ctrl snaps a guide's handle | Blender, Toon Boom | v1 |
| Select points with box and lasso; transform points | Both | v1 |
| Selection modes on 1, 2, 3 (points, edges, strokes) and L to select a whole shape | Blender | v1 |
| Split Curve tool: click a line to add a point without changing its shape | Community | v1 |
| Knife tool (Ctrl+K): cut across shapes to add internal segments and split fills | Blender | v1 |
| Detail levels per shape (multiresolution shapes): edit a shape with fewer points while its finer detail rides along; the knife adds detail at higher levels (see below) | Blender, Open Animate | v1 |
| G, R, S modal move, rotate, scale with X/Y/Z axis lock and typed values (see Viewport toolbar) | Blender | v1 |
| E to extrude a new point from an end point | Blender | v1 |
| X on selected points opens the Delete menu, as Blender's: Points (the curve joins straight across each gap, and a shape along it stays, as Moho keeps it; a filled shape goes only when its outline no longer goes round, as a loop down to two points; a line keeps its stroke on the segments left when its end points are deleted, and goes only with its last segment; a point whose curve goes with it (the other end of a line of two) stays, on no curve, as Blender's Delete Vertices leaves the other end of an edge), Edges (the segments between selected points go, curves splitting there; a point left on no curve goes too, as Blender's Delete Edges takes the vertices it leaves loose, while points still on a curve stay) and Dissolve Points (the curve joins across each gap, so lines and shapes stay whole, and keeps its shape: each joined segment's two handles keep their directions and take the lengths that fit it closest to the curve as it was, by Schneider's least-squares fit with Newton steps, as Blender's Dissolve Vertices fits curves; a run of neighbouring points is fitted as one span. Every other point of an 8-point circle leaves the outline within 0.4% of its width, against 8.6% unfitted. Detail points riding on the level below are removed as Delete Higher removes them, so the knife's points, dissolved before they are moved, give back the curve exactly: the knife records each cut as an exact split of the segment below, even several cuts in one segment, rather than guessing its place from the length along the curve). The Delete key deletes the selected points at once. F (Make Segment, as Blender's for curves) joins the two selected points with a segment when each is the end of an open curve: ends of two curves join them into one (a curve drawn the other way round is turned to fit), and the two ends of one curve close it. The handles stay as they were (one with no length leans a third of the way along the new segment). A shape whose outline stops at a joined end (one of its runs ends there: a shape of several runs too) runs on along the new segment; two such shapes, one each side, become one (the first's name and look kept); a shape that then goes all the way round is filled. Any other two points (Blender's F between any two vertices) get a segment of their own, welded to both, drawn as the line through either is (a line's shape takes it as another run; next to a filled shape, a line-only shape in its look, so the fill keeps going round); two points already joined, or one point: nothing happens and the status bar says why. One undo step; Python: oanim.ops.mesh.make_segment() | Blender | v1 |
| Mirror editing (X and Y in the viewport header, saved with the file: opening one sets them as saved, a new file has both off, and toggling one is a change to save): moving a point moves its mirror image across the layer's axis too. Points are paired by where they are in the setup pose (frame 0), so the pairs hold on every frame, and the image snaps to the reflected spot (handles too), making the sides symmetrical on any frame. With X on, a layer named for a side (Ear_L, Ear.R, L_Ear, Left..., the names bones pair by) mirrors its point edits into its partner layer (Ear_R: one beside it, else anywhere) instead of across its own axis: the two mirror across the document's vertical axis (a bone layer's, inside one), as Symmetrize Layers makes them, each point pairing with the partner's point nearest its reflection in the setup pose (within 2% of the partner's size), handles too. It follows G, R and S, Slide, Smooth Points, Mirror, handle drags, Align and the Transform Points tool, and away from frame 0 keys the partner's pose; a partner's selected points move by themselves only while that layer is being edited too. Layer moves don't mirror. A layer without a side keeps mirroring inside itself. With X on, the rest of editing mirrors too. Operators on the selection (Separate, Delete, Dissolve, Delete Edges, Delete Shape, Duplicate, Arrange, Set Brush Shape, Link Material, Scale Radius (Alt S, its images widening with the drag as it goes), Clear Location and Handles, the detail level moves, point group Assign and Remove) act on the selected points' mirror images as well: in the same layer, or in a sided layer's partner, which is edited along for that run and then left as it was picked; the selection is put back after. Both a point and its image selected (both sides picked, or a duplicate of both), the -x one follows the other, reflected. A shape's look changed (fill, outline, colours, width, brush, material, from Properties or anywhere) goes to its mirror shape when the step is recorded, unless that one changed too. New geometry is mirrored when its step is recorded: a new curve (Draw, Pen, Shape tool) gets a reflected copy with its shapes; a knife cut cuts the image segment at the reflected spot; points grown from an open end (Extrude, the pen going on) grow the image curve's end; a curve closed (F) closes its image; a new shape over existing curves is made over the image segments. Nothing is made twice (an image already there is left), detail points stay on their own side, and steps that rebuild a drawing (a Boolean applied) aren't mirrored. A new layer or group made inside a group named for a side (Arm_L) makes its twin in the partner group (Arm_R) at the same place, the two named for their sides in the groups' way (Layer 3_L, Layer 3_R) so they pair from then on (numbered so neither side's name is taken); the one made in the group worked in stays active. Reordering a sided group's layers (dragging, Raise, Lower) reorders the partner group's to match, each going where its counterpart went; layers without one keep their places. Renaming a sided layer or group (Ear_L to Lobe_L) renames its partner to match (Lobe_R), when the new name has a side and that name is free. | Blender | v1 |
| Curvature, smooth and peak points | Moho | v1 |
| Create shape, fill and outline, shared looks (Materials: see below) | Moho, Blender | v1 |
| Line width per point, every line has round corners and round ends, hide edge, delete edge | Moho | v1 |
| Sculpt tool (Blender's Grease Pencil sculpt brushes, for curves; Ctrl Tab switches to it and back to the tool before, wherever the mouse is, as Tab toggles the setup frame (Tab and Shift Tab hop between fields only while one is being typed in; ImGui's own Ctrl Tab window switcher is off); Python: oanim.ops.wm.tool_sculpt_toggle(), oanim.wm.tool()): Grab, Push, Smooth, Surface Smooth, Thickness (Ctrl: thins), Pinch (Ctrl: Inflate) and Twist (Ctrl: the other way); holding Shift as a stroke starts smooths instead, whatever the brush (the built-in Smooth's strength and falloff at this brush's size, as Blender's sculpt modes), and Alt-click still picks a layer; with Radius (screen pixels; F sizes it with the mouse, as Blender's radial control: the circle stays centred where the brush is and the pointer doesn't move; moving right grows it, left shrinks it, Shift for Precision Mode (a tenth as fast); click or Enter keeps it, Esc or right-click puts it back), Strength (Shift F, the same way), Falloff (Smooth, Sphere, Constant), pen pressure for either, and Selected Only (Blender's selection mask); points don't show while it is the tool unless Selected Only is on (as Blender's sculpt modes with the selection mask). The brushes work on the line, not just its points: when a stroke starts each curve is resampled at even steps along its length (a few per brush width, at least four a segment; each sample keeps its place in its segment's own parameter, so the fit gives an untouched segment back exactly), each point anchored to its own; the brush moves the samples, and after every dab the points are fitted back to them (each point goes where its sample went, the handles of each segment the brush touched are fitted to the samples between its points by least squares, a smooth point's handles that were in line stay so and a corner stays a corner; a stroke that barely moves leaves the line exactly as it was, bones and detail levels included); segments it didn't touch keep their points and handles exactly. The samples last the whole stroke, so fitting never builds on its own error, and only the fitted curve shows. Thickness changes the width along the samples and each point takes the average change around it. It works where the points show (through the bones and deforms: on a frame a deform bends, each dab's points are settled until they show where the brush put them, as a move's are), on every layer being edited, on the detail level being edited (finer detail rides along), with X mirror: a layer without a side (an _X layer) is sculpted on both sides of its own axis at once, each dab also made mirrored there (Twist turning the other way), as Blender's symmetry; what mirrors is the change, so a pose that isn't symmetric (a turned head) keeps its lean: each point's image takes the mirrored change of the side under the brush (handles and widths too), and a point on the axis moves only along it, its handles changing as each other's mirror image; points pair with their images where they rest in the setup pose, whatever is selected. A side's layer passes its change into its partner as moves do (Ear_L into Ear_R; Thickness's widths too); without Dyntopo it never adds points; away from frame 0 it poses the drawing (auto-key keys it, and it records while playing), on frame 0 it edits the setup (Keep Poses as for moves); a stroke is one undo step. While it is the tool a brush shelf runs along the bottom of the viewport (Blender's asset shelf; the view sits above it): tabs All, Move, Shape and Mine, a search field, the tiles' size, a fold arrow and a top edge to drag; each tile is a preset (brush, radius, strength, falloff) drawn as a line before and after the brush, its name beneath; a brush keeps what it is changed to (radius, strength, falloff) while Open Animate runs, as a Blender brush keeps its settings: going to another and back brings the changes back, not its saved settings (its tooltip shows them; saving it as a preset starts it afresh); built-in: Grab, Grab Soft, Grab Big, Push, Pinch, Twist, Smooth, Smooth Strong, Surface Smooth, Thickness; the + tile saves the brush as it is now to the asset library (<library>/Sculpt Brushes/<Name>.oasculpt, under Mine), right-click deletes a saved one. Keys while it is the tool: G Grab, P Push, S Smooth, T Thickness, I Pinch, W Twist. Smooth and Surface Smooth both work on while the brush is held, moving or not (each frame is its share of a dab, a tenth of a second a whole one). Smooth is Blender's Grease Pencil Smooth on the resampled line: each dab blurs the samples with Blender's binomial kernel (gaussian_blur_1D, two iterations, ends kept, no keep shape), each moving strength x falloff of the way to its blurred place; as in Blender, a straight line stays straight while its points may slide along it, and a curve brushed long enough relaxes and shrinks. Surface Smooth moves the line toward its smoothed self: when a stroke starts, each curve is resampled at even steps along its length and noise-reduced by the Whittaker-Eilers smoother of the third order (Whittaker 1923; Eilers 2003: the line nearest the samples whose change of bend is least, one banded linear solve), which takes out wiggles shorter than its cutoff while an arc, a bend or a long wave keeps its shape; corners (a point not set smooth that is peaked or whose handles bend more than 25 degrees; a point set smooth never is, whatever its handles, so extruded points with handles of no length and sharp kinks between them are smoothed) and open ends stay put, and a loop is smoothed all round. Then each of the curve's own points goes where its place along the line went and each segment's handles are fitted to the smoothed line between its two points (their directions the smoothed line's), so the line with all its points traces the smoothed one; nothing is added or removed. While held, each point and its handles go strength x falloff x the frame's share of the way there, so the effect builds up and stops at the fit, never past it; an arc or a circle comes out as it was. While it is the brush, the fit shows as a pale ghost curve wherever it differs from the line by more than a pixel and a half (during a stroke the stroke's own fit, else the fit a stroke would start with now; after a Surface Smooth stroke it keeps that stroke's targets, since smoothing the smoothed line again lands in the same place; it is worked out again only after an edit, a stroke, undo or redo, or when the frame, the layers or the Lump Sensitivity change, and then only for the curves that changed (while Lump Sensitivity is being scrubbed only those the view shows, so it keeps up, and every editable curve once the scrubbing stops, so moving or zooming the view later finds them all fitted), never for moving or zooming the view, and a stroke starts from it rather than fitting again; it shows whether or not the mouse is over the view, so it follows Lump Sensitivity as that is scrubbed in the header), and the header shows its Lump Sensitivity, 0-1 (a slider, default 0.25, dragged within 0.001-1, typed as high as wanted or down to 0, which takes out nothing) for how long a wiggle along the line it takes out: 1 is a quarter of the canvas's height, so wiggles up to about that long go however tall they are, while longer waves stay; the result changes smoothly as it is scrubbed; neither the file's resolution, the brush's size nor the zoom changes it. The ghost follows it as it changes. Python: surface_sensitivity in oanim.wm.set_sculpt, sensitivity= on sculpt.stroke. Surface Smooth has no key. Dyntopo (Blender's dynamic topology, for lines): a Dyntopo toggle with a checkbox and a popover sits at the right end of the tool settings row while Sculpt is the tool, apart from the brush's own settings on the left, as it is the same for every brush (as Blender's tool header). With it on, on a drawing without animation, Grab, Push, Pinch and Twist resample the line before they move it: each segment a dab is about to move (a brush that reaches none of it moves none), and the segments either side of one, get a point at each of the stroke's own samples, a sixth of the brush's radius apart as without Dyntopo, whatever the Resolution (so the line follows the brush as closely as it would without Dyntopo; a segment shorter than that gets none), the Bezier split there exactly so the line doesn't move, on a posed frame too (a new point is weighted between its neighbours and stored where those weights show it on the line). From then on the dab moves those points, each handle turning and stretching as the line to its neighbour did (a smooth point's two by the same turn, so they stay in line); nothing is fitted while dragging, so nothing jumps. When the stroke ends, with Decimate ticked in the popover (off by default; off, every point the stroke added stays), Collapse takes out every point the stroke made or moved that the line keeps its shape without, cheapest first, each span left fitted as one segment whose handles keep their directions. A span counts as close enough only when it is within that tolerance of the line as it showed both ways round, so it can't bulge. Collapse never takes out an open curve's end, a corner, a point two curves share, or one of the drawing's own points that is in a point group, has weights of its own or (Selected Only) isn't selected. A point the stroke made takes its bone weights and point groups between its neighbours', and is selected when both of them are. Resolution (50 by default, Blender's Constant Detail) sets only how closely Decimate and Simplify keep the line, never the points a stroke adds: points per unit of the layer's own space, following the layer's scale and its groups', never the zoom, the canvas or the brush; Collapse keeps the line within a tenth of a step where it shows (each span fitted there, so a squashed or turned layer keeps it too), and its width within a twentieth of the width there. While it is being set (scrubbed in the popover, or R over the canvas: right doubles it every 120 pixels, left halves it, a click keeps it, Esc puts it back) a ghost grid of its steps covers the view, drawn in the active layer's space (scaled, turned and stretched with it), fading out half a second after; a grid too fine to draw (under two pixels) says so instead. Simplify, in the popover, runs Collapse over the whole drawing at the Resolution (sculpt.simplify(resolution=)), each layer by its own scale; its Adjust Last Operation panel has the Resolution and says how many points went. The stroke's new shape becomes the drawing's setup pose on any frame and makes no keys, even with auto-key on. Dyntopo is off, with the reason in its tooltip and popover, on a layer that is a reference or has one, has detail levels, has its points or handles keyed (the main timeline or any action), or while X Mirror is on (mirror pairs go by where points rest: not yet). A stroke is one undo step, Collapse included. It stays quick: on the eye rig, a 120-dab Grab took 10 to 16 ms with Dyntopo at Resolution 50 or 200, Collapse included, and 10 to 15 ms without; Collapse, Simplify and the resample take about as long per point however many points there are (points about as cheap to take out are tried in a scrambled order, so spans grow from many places at once). Python: dyntopo, decimate and resolution in oanim.wm.set_sculpt, oanim.wm.sculpt_dyntopo_blocked(layer), oanim.wm.sculpt_dyntopo_cell(layer, resolution) (a grid cell's sides as it shows), resolution= and decimate= on sculpt.stroke. Python: oanim.ops.sculpt.stroke(path="x,y;x,y", brush=, radius=, strength=, falloff=, invert=, selected_only=, mirror=), sculpt.preset_load / preset_save / preset_remove(name=), sculpt.brush_set(brush=); oanim.wm.sculpt_settings(), set_sculpt(), sculpt_presets() | Blender | v1 |
| More sculpting brushes: randomize, clone; auto-masking by shape or layer, custom falloff curves | Blender | Later |
| Slide point along curve, point reduction | Community | Later |
| Gradients and texture fills | Moho | Later |
| Eraser and scatter brush | Moho | Later |
| Liquid shapes: combine shapes by add, subtract, intersect | Moho | Maybe |
| Pen tablet support through Windows Ink (Microsoft's pen API, current and still developed) or WinTab (the tablet makers' older one, which comes with a tablet's driver, not with Windows), picked by Tablet API in Preferences: Automatic uses Windows Ink and WinTab when the pen sends nothing through that (a driver with Use Windows Ink off), a driver sending both being heard through Windows Ink; a WinTab pick with no driver installed uses Windows Ink, and Preferences says so. WinTab reads the tablet's own coordinates and maps them to the screen itself, since drivers get high-DPI and several displays wrong, and believes the cursor where the two disagree by more than 40 pixels. Either way: every sample the pen sends, not one mouse position a frame: a Windows message hook reads each pointer message's history (GetPointerPenInfoHistory, oldest first), with each sample's pressure, tilt, time and end, at the sub-pixel place the tablet gives, so a quick stroke keeps its every turn (a zigzag inside one frame comes out whole, where the mouse alone gives a straight line). The Draw tool takes the samples of the pen's touch from where it touched down, even when the press reaches it a frame late, and while drawing runs the line 8 ms ahead of the pen the way it is going (Prediction; none while the pen moves slowly, at most 40 pixels), so it keeps up with the hand. The eraser end draws nothing with the Draw tool (the erasers will use it) and erases notes with Annotate Draw and Line, as Ctrl does. Python: oanim.wm.pen() (in range, touching, eraser, pressure, tilt, the touch's samples), oanim.wm.draw_settings() / set_draw(prediction=, use_pressure=, fill=), mouse_simulate(pen=[(x, y, pressure, tilt_x, tilt_y, seconds)], eraser=) | Both | v1 |
| Stamp brushes: brush shapes and brushes (presets) in the asset library, Moho's brush settings including line boil, Photoshop's dynamics (see below) | Moho | v1 |

**Draw tools.** One Draw tool on the toolbar, and while it is the tool a shelf along the bottom of the viewport, as Sculpt's (tabs All, Pens, Erasers, Trim, Mine): Stroke Pen (freehand lines, a centre line with width), Fill Pen (paints filled areas, merging with a fill of the same look it overlaps, as Illustrator's Blob Brush), Bezier Pen (today's click-to-place pen), Stroke Eraser (cuts lines where brushed, the new ends capped round; fills untouched; Touched part or Whole line), Fill Eraser (cuts the brushed area out of fills; lines untouched) and Vector Trimmer (brush across a line: the touched piece goes, out to the nearest crossings, as Clip Studio's Erase up to intersection). The shelf and header (stage 3, done): tabs with nothing in them don't show, so Erasers and Trim come with their tools. The pens are Ink, Pencil, Marker, Tapered, Mouse and the Bezier Pen, plus saved ones under Mine (the + tile; <library>/Draw Presets/<Name>.oadraw); each tile's picture is a sample line the engine makes from that pen's settings. A preset holds the line's width and how the line is made, not colours. A pen keeps what it was changed to while the app runs, marked with a dot on its tile and * in the header; right-click a tile to Put Back or Delete. Shift Space or the header's pen button shows the same tiles at the mouse, for anyone who folds the shelf.
