| Copy, paste and paste-flipped poses using \_L and \_R bone names | Blender, community | v1 |
| Weight painting: a Weight Paint tool in the toolbar for layers inside a bone layer, working like Blender's (active bone, weight, radius, strength, Draw/Add/Subtract, normalize; Ctrl subtracts, Ctrl-click picks the bone; curve handles are hidden while painting or selecting weights) | Blender | v1 |
| Pose library: save poses and expressions as thumbnails, apply with one click | Blender | Later |
| Setup toggle: jump to frame 0 and back, with clear on-screen signals and rigging tools limited to frame 0 (see Core concepts) | Moho, Blender | v1 |
| Bone constraints in a Bone Constraints tab beside Armature (the active bone's, while it is selected; with none selected it asks for one), as Blender's stack (add, reorder, on/off, remove, animatable Influence with its key button; on/off can be driven, and then shows purple, names its driver on hover and has the driver menu on right-click, a click leaving it to the driver): Limit Angle, Independent Angle, Maintain Volume (the width follows the length however it got there, keeping the area), Copy Angle / Position / Scale, Track To, Stretch To, IK, Dynamics, Limit to Shape, Child Of, Look At, Follow Point. They act on posed frames, never on the setup frame. Limit Angle's Min and Max are turns from the bone's setup angle, the same numbers the Bone panel's Angle shows while posing (so they move with the rest pose; older files' angles convert), each with a Set to Current Angle button that takes the bone's Angle now (Python: oanim.ops.armature.limit_angle_set_current(index=, end=MIN|MAX)); it shows, as Moho does, a red line from a selected bone's base at its smallest angle and one at its largest. Dynamics, last in the stack, swings from where the constraints above it put the bone: a Limit Angle limits its aim, not its swing. **Limit to Shape** (Add Bone Constraint > Limit): the bone's head stays inside a closed shape of another layer (picked from a list with a search field, as Parent Bone's, or with its eyedropper by clicking the shape in the viewport) as that shape is drawn on each frame (bent by its bones, moved by its layers), so a pupil stays inside its eye white however the head turns and the eye narrows. Keep Inside: Center (the head) or Whole Drawing (all of what the bone carries fits: a layer, or Auto, the layers following the bone); Overlap lets that go part way past the edge (an eyelid or mask covers it); Softness eases it to a stop short of the edge instead of hitting it. It holds after Dynamics too, so a jiggle can't push it out; it slides along the edge (the nearest place that fits); inside the shape it does nothing. Affect Transform (on, as Blender's): moving the bone past the edge stops its own position there too, so its keys hold where it shows; off, only where it shows is held. Its shape is outlined in red while the bone is selected. Overlap and Softness can be driven; saved with the file. Python: constraint_add(type=LIMIT_SHAPE), bone_constraint_set(shape_layer=, shape=, fit=CENTER|WHOLE, fit_layer=, overlap=, softness=, affect_transform=). **Child Of** (Blender's, Relationship): the bone moves, turns and scales with its target's change from its setup pose as if parented to it (Position, Rotation, Scale each switchable; no Set Inverse needed); key Influence to pick the target up or drop it. **Follow Point** (Harmony's Point Kinematic Output, Relationship): the bone stays on a bending shape without bending. Pick a Curve, Cage or Mesh Deform, on a drawing or a group; the constraint keeps a point of its own, starting at the bone's head, and the bone takes that point's change from frame 0 on top of what its parent gives it, so nothing jumps and keys on the bone still work. Points says how many places it goes by (Harmony's Sampling Type). One Point moves the bone; with Rotation (off on a new constraint, and on one-point constraints from files before it) it also turns as the shape turns there: an Own Place point as the bend twists around it, a point of the shape as the curve's direction through that point turns (Harmony's Kinematic Output). Two Points: the way from the first to the second turns the bone (Rotation), and how far apart they grow scales it (Scale), each switchable; Scale is Uniform (the whole bone grows, as before) or Along Line, which stretches it along the line through the points and squashes it across that line by (1 / stretch - 1) x Volume + 1 (Harmony's Two-Point Sampling with Scaling: Volume 1 keeps its area, 0 doesn't squash, more squashes further). Three Points: the bone takes the whole change the three make, turn, both scales and skew (Harmony's Three-Point Sampling), so Rotation and Scale are greyed out; its angle takes the turn and a shape change on the bone's pose, applied after everything else, takes the rest, both blended by Influence. As in Harmony, three points exactly on a line don't move the bone at all, and nearly on a line they skew it a lot; the panel says which. The turn, scale and shape change all go round the first point where it rested, as Harmony's go round its first pivot, so a head away from that point swings round it (checked against Harmony 24's Point Kinematic Output and Kinematic Output on 185 measured cases, to 1e-5). Harmony's guards are kept too: a point within 0.0032 of the first is read 1e-4 further along (the second along x, the third up, then across), the line between two points counts as at least 1e-4 long at rest and 1e-5 now, and one bent to nothing points along x. Fewer points keeps the others for later. Files from before load as One or Two Points, with Uniform scale. Show Follow Point shows the point, light blue, and hiding it changes nothing. Drag it on frame 0 to choose the place that's followed (the bone stays put); hold Ctrl while dragging to snap it to the nearest point of the deform's shape, ringed in white, and let go there to make it that point of the shape (dragging a point of the shape on frame 0 gives it its own place again, where it is); drag it on any other frame, or set Shift, to key it, and it lands under the mouse through the bend: a follower slides along the shape. Its keys hold the change from setup, so moving it on frame 0 moves it on every frame. Each bone has its own point, so two eyes can sit on one cage. Each point's row (Point, Second, Third) says Own Place, or a point the deform's shape already has, picked from its list or with the eyedropper, as Follow Deform's: the bone takes that point's change from frame 0, by the deform's Strength, with nothing to drag or shift (Python: follow_shape_point=, follow_shape_point2=, follow_shape_point3= in bone_constraint_set, a point's number on the shape's layer, None for its own place; follow_points= 1 to 3, follow_point3=, follow_shift3=, follow_scale_mode= UNIFORM or ALONG_LINE, follow_volume=; it reads follow_shape, the shape change as (a, b, c, d)). A new deform lets go of it, and a point deleted from the shape changes nothing, the row saying Point gone. A layer follows by Parent to Bone, or with no bone by a Follow Deform modifier. A bone above it, or the bones carrying the deform's layer, moving the shape too count once. A bone that moves what it follows, directly or through parents, targets, IK or other followers, is refused with a message in the panel; followers that read each other go in order. Symmetrize mirrors the point, as its own place, and moves it to the other side's deform when the layer has a partner (Brow_L, Brow_R). Action bone actions, drivers, Dynamics and Delay don't see the move. **Look At** (Tracking), for eyes: the target bone is a look target standing in front of the face at its **Depth** (a keyable field in its Bone panel); each eye a stretched ball as big as its Eye Shape at frame 0, turning to look at it, measured from the rest (target where it rests, at its setup Depth: the eyes look as drawn). Close, the eyes cross; far, they look the same way. The pupil bone only moves: to where the pupil points on the ball, seen from the front, never past the side of the ball, and the drawings following it go with it as drawn. The pupil's own keys move it on the ball, not the ball, and its Eye Shape keeps where the pupil actually is inside. Its **Eye Shape** (Limit to Shape's rows inside it: the layer from a searchable list or its eyedropper, which shape, Center or Whole Drawing, Overlap, Softness) says how big the eye is and keeps the pupil inside the eye white, so it stops at the edge however far the target goes. With no Eye Shape, Look At does nothing and its panel asks for one. It works at any head angle (worked out along the face's axes, as the eye's parent has turned). While the bone is selected, a faint blue line runs from the eye to its target and its Eye Shape is outlined in red, as Limit to Shape's. Look At and Limit to Shape refuse a shape their own bone moves (weighted to it or to a bone hanging on it, on it by Parent Bone, bent by a deform it moves, or turned by an IK on it whose chain carries the shape), as Follow Point refuses such a deform: the bone would chase itself, the pose depending on the frames shown before and evaluating stalling; refused, it does nothing and says so in red ("Loop: this bone moves its Eye Shape", shape_error in Python; `layer_moved_by_bone` in `src/anim/follow_point.cc`), and works again once the loop is let go of. The eye rig: a look target following the head through Child Of, and Look At on each pupil with its eye white as its Eye Shape. Python: constraint_add(type=CHILD_OF|LOOK_AT), bone_constraint_set(child_position=, child_rotation=, child_scale=, and Limit to Shape's shape_layer= ... for its Eye Shape), bone_set(depth=) | Blender, Moho | v1 |

**Animation and timeline**

| Feature | From | Tier |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Animated channels with every Moho interpolation mode, holds and on-twos. Frame 0's key is the setup (the rest pose, in the mainline and every action): deleting keys (Alt I, the timeline, a key button, Python's keyframe_delete) takes it only with the last of a channel's keys, since without it frame 0 would show the nearest key's pose and recording there would take that pose as the rest | Moho | v1 |
| Timeline and dope sheet: select, move, scale, copy and paste keys. **Ignore Bones** (Duplicate Keys and Paste Keys): each copied point and stored handle is keyed again so it shows on the new frame where it did on the frame it was copied from, whatever the bones do there (bones keyed in the same copy move with it; handles the curve works out follow their points; detail points follow their parents); off by default, kept from the last time it was set, greyed out when no bones move the layers' points. **Keep Place** (the same two): only the shape is copied, the copied points moving together so their centre shows on the new frame where it was there before (the pieces copied together keep how they sit with each other); off by default, kept from the last time it was set (Python: anim.keys_duplicate(frames=, ignore_bones=, keep_place=), anim.keys_paste(ignore_bones=, keep_place=)). Shift D ends as Duplicate Keys with the offset the mouse gave, so the timeline's Adjust Last Operation panel (bottom-left, as the viewport's) shows Offset, Ignore Bones and Keep Place to change; Paste Keys shows Ignore Bones and Keep Place there. Pasted on frame 0, the copied pose becomes the setup pose as a move there with Keep Poses does: the other frames keep showing the poses they had; one undo step, Paste Keys as the Setup Pose. Double-click a row off its keys on a frame to key its channels there: the playhead goes to that frame and what it shows is keyed (a layer's row keys everything it has, a group's row such as Bones or Points its kind, a property's row that property, the Selected Points row only the selected points), one undo step (Insert Keys in Row); Python: oanim.ops.anim.keys_insert(frame=, layers=, kinds=, selected_only=, path=, pinned=). Channel rows follow the Layers editor, as Blender's Dope Sheet follows the outliner: Summary first (every key below it, on a band of its own; when the timeline shows one layer alone, Summary and its row are one, "Summary / <layer>", its channels under it); Pinned (red: properties pinned with the pin button beside their key button in Properties, listed whatever is selected, even with no keys left, each with its pin to unpin it; View > Clear Pins unpins all); then each shown layer in the Layers editor's order and nesting, folding to its own channels (Position, Rotation, Scale, Shear, Opacity, Visibility, Draw Order, Switch, masks), its Drawing (keyed as a whole; red while its points are picked) or Points (with the Selected Points under it in red), its Bones (each bone a folder of its Angle, Transform, Scale, Depth and constraints' settings; picked bones red; a linked controller's knob named with its bone) and a Media layer's clips, then the layers inside it; the camera and the document's Simulation come last. The picked layers start open and the rest folded; the project keeps each fold. What is picked opens: picking points in a drawing or bones in a bone layer opens that layer, the groups it is in, its Points or Bones folder and the picked bones, and turns the layer's name red (as Blender's Dope Sheet shows the selected channels); folded while the pick lasts, a row stays folded, and once it is gone each row goes back to the project's fold, which a pick never changes (oanim.wm.timeline_row_fold(row, open) folds as the arrow does). Clicking a layer's name makes it the active layer. Over the names column: Only Selected (the pointer, on for new documents: the picked layers (the active one unless deselected) and those whose points or bones are picked while being edited, each with its name row even without keys, the animated layers inside a group with Edit Descendants (a bone layer's only when picked themselves, as Blender's Only Show Selected) and the groups they are in, dimmed; of a bone layer only the picked bones, its row and the Summary showing its own keys and theirs (no bone picked, no bones); nothing picked, no rows (pinned ones aside) and a grey "Nothing selected: pick a layer, bones or points"; off, every animated layer), Lock (the padlock, anim.timeline_lock: keeps the layers shown when it was turned on, whatever is picked after), the Filters menu and a search (anim.timeline_filter(show=, kinds=, search=, only_selected=, clear=)): show Animated (rows for channels with keys, the default), Changed (keys that don't all hold one value) or All (every channel, keys or not); kinds Transform, Bones, Points, Opacity, Switch, Media and Other; the search matches names fuzzily (the letters in order, any case), a layer's or bone's name finding all it holds, and opens folded rows while it is typed. A red "N hidden" under the rows counts what the filters take away; clicking it clears them. All of these are saved with the project. The names column's edge drags to widen it; long names end in an ellipsis and show whole on hover. Rows without keys are hidden (pinned ones and All aside); a folding row shows the keys of all it holds. Keys: a frame whose keys are all fillers (Key Together's, Key Whole Drawing's) is grey and hollow; keys outside the playback range are faint. Mouse: click a key to pick it, Shift-click adds (or takes out), Ctrl-click takes out, double-click picks every key of its row and the rows inside it; drag a key (past 4 pixels) to move the selected keys, Ctrl while dragging for Ripple, Alt for just the one channel, let go to confirm; pressing a selected key among others and letting go picks it alone. Numbers typed set the move during G, S, Shift D or a drag (minus flips the sign, Backspace takes back). Over the keys the wheel zooms around the mouse, Ctrl+wheel and middle-drag pan and Shift+wheel scrolls the rows (over the names the wheel scrolls them), and the scroll bar under the keys pans (the bar) and zooms (its ends, never closer than 10 frames). Keys draw over the playhead's line, so a key on the frame shown stays in sight. The header's right end holds the action being edited (or Mainline), then the current frame, then Start and End. Python: oanim.wm.timeline_rows(), timeline_row_details(as_shown=) (each row's depth, name, frames, filler_frames, fold and open), timeline_hidden(), timeline_key_press(row, frame, to_frame, ctrl=, shift=, double_click=, ripple=, alt=, typed=), timeline_scrollbar_drag(). Tab goes to the setup frame (0) and back to the frame you came from; with the mouse over the viewport it always does, never hopping between text fields (one being edited lets go). Click a layer's name to make it active; click or box-select keys in any rows, each row picking only its own kind of key. Ctrl C / Ctrl V copy and paste keys at the current frame, onto the same layers' channels, even into another action. Copied in a 2 Axis Controller side's own action, the knob's keys there and its linked dial's turns are that side's timing, not a move: they paste only back into that action, never onto the mainline or another action (where they would play the action on top of the pose pasted with them), and the first key that does paste lands on the current frame. On the layer they came from, a point's keys go back on that same point wherever it is now (a point added or deleted before it changes its place), and none go anywhere if it was deleted. Keyed Here: Right-click a key for its menu, Interpolation first, then a Keyed on frame N submenu (greyed out where nothing is keyed) with every property keyed on that frame under its row, by owner, with its value and interpolation (points grouped by kind, each group listing its points; held still over a key, the same list shows as a tooltip); hovering an item outlines its bone or points in the viewport, and choosing one jumps to that frame (Alt stays) and selects its bone, or its points or handles (staying in the layer worked in when it can edit them, else going to theirs; Shift adds), or opens it in the Properties editor, its panel open and its row flashing blue for a second (Select These Keys was taken out: a click on the key picks them) | Blender | v1 |

| Actions and the mainline; insert an action as a copy or reference | Moho | v1 |

| Drivers: as Blender drives any number, checkbox, dropdown or colour of the scene's data (not text, links to other data, or tool and editor settings), any of Open Animate's can be worked out each frame from other values: every keyable value (a layer's transform, opacity and visibility, the camera, the Simulation settings, a bone's angle, position and scale, constraint settings and Influence), a shape's Position / Transform row (what it shows: on frame 0 the middle of its points, on other frames how far that has moved from the setup pose; driving it moves them together), Fill and Outline, Fill and Line Color and Line Width (in pixels, as shown; for shapes sharing a material these are the material's, so the driver and its purple show on every shape that shares it), a layer's Show in Renders, Locked, Clip to Below and media look and sound, its mask rows' mode, invert, grow and extend, the Mirror modifier's settings, a bone's IK settings, every constraint setting, and the document's Background. Checkboxes are on from 0.5; whole numbers and dropdowns round. Right-clicking such a field offers Add Driver..., Copy As New Driver (a driver that reads this field), Paste Driver (several numbers copied onto a field with another count, as a Scale's X and Y onto one number, list each as its own Paste Driver: look: Scale Y; Python: anim.driver_paste(part=)), and on a driven field Edit Driver..., Copy Driver and Delete Driver; a colour's paste reaches every selected shape. Typing #expression into a number field (#frame/10) drives it. A driven field is purple, red while its driver doesn't work (hovering says why). The Driver window edits one: Expression, Average, Sum, Min or Max; variables, each reading a layer's, the camera's or the Simulation's value (one part of a pair, X or Y); Remap from one range onto another, clamped or not; the value or what's wrong. Expressions are Open Animate's own fast language (Python-like: + - * / % **, comparisons, and/or/not, a if test else b, frame, pi, sin...smoothstep), never Python, compiled once. Drivers run in order of what they read, a loop stops the ones in it, driven values are never keyed and are held to their field's range (Opacity 0 to 1), and they save with the file and undo. As Blender's do, they follow what they drive: a renamed bone, a constraint, modifier or mask row moved or another removed; deleting what one drives deletes it, and duplicating a layer copies its drivers. On the setup frame (0) drivers leave bones and shapes at rest, as constraints do, so no driven pose becomes the setup; a driven action bone plays its action bone action at once. Expressions follow Python: a < b < c, x or 0.5 (the deciding value), and a variable may take the name of frame, pi or e | Blender | v1 |
| A Drivers editor listing every driver in the file | Blender | Later |
| Action strips layered on the timeline | Blender | Maybe |
| Frame-by-frame drawing: a vector layer whose drawing changes per frame, with onion skins | Both | v1 |
| Auto-keying record button, on by default. Moving something while the animation plays records it, as Blender does: each frame played keys where the drag holds it, everything else goes on playing its own animation, and the whole recording is one undo step. A dropdown beside it, as Blender's: Add & Replace (the default: whatever changes is keyed, adding keys where there are none) or Replace (only keys already on the frame change; a change elsewhere isn't keyed), and Moho's Auto-Freeze Keys (keying a bone keys every other bone of its bone layer shown there on that frame, angle, position and scale, and keying a point keys every point of its drawing, where it is, its width, curvature and handles, so the whole pose and shape hold there; not with Replace). Saved with the project; Python: document.auto_key_mode ("ADD_REPLACE", "REPLACE"), document.auto_freeze_keys | Blender, Moho | v1 |
| **Key Whole Drawing** (Layer tab > Layer, vector layers; on for new ones, off for layers in files made before it): the drawing is keyed as a whole, as one movement. An edit on a frame keys every point there (position, width, curvature and handles), the untouched ones holding where they are, so the drawing is keyed as poses; a point added later follows its curve; detail points keep no keys, as ever. Deleting one point's key deletes that frame from the whole drawing (deleting a point takes only its own keys). The timeline shows its keys as one Drawing row instead of Points. Turned on for a drawing keyed point by point, every point is keyed wherever any is, holding what it has there, and on each frame the keys take the timing most of them have (interpolation, hold, speeds...); the Info editor says how many changed. That happens once, as it is turned on: a point timed apart since keeps its timing, opening the file too. Turned off, the keys stay. Bone deforms and actions apply as before. With the Motion Path tool its points trace no path; the timing chart and Path Timing graph have a “<layer> drawing” row whose stretches run between its poses, and every timing edit there (sliding dots, Shift's blend, the shape buttons, Along and Speed, overshoot) applies to all its channels; the layer's own row is left out when it doesn't move itself, and until a row is picked on the graph shows the first with timing. Saved with the file. Python: layer.key_whole_drawing; the motion path operators take drawing=1 with layer=. Later: an onion-skin preview of the poses. | Moho, Blender | v1 |
| **Key Together** (Layer tab > Layer, groups and bone layers; off for new ones): everything in the group is keyed as one movement, each layer's move, turn, size and opacity, its bones and its drawing's points (a group inside it with it: one already on hands its keys over, its fillers kept so nothing moves, and keys its own again once the outer one is off). Turned on, every layer is keyed wherever any is, the keys added only to hold a layer where it was being fillers (one inside motion the layer already has, or after its last key of its own, shapes no curve, so turning it on moves nothing, and one inside a stretch with a timing curve (Custom) or control points goes in where its dot is, the stretch keeping its way and timing, as Key Whole Drawing's do; one before a key it gets later holds it there), and on each frame the keys take the timing most of them have (the Info editor says how many changed). While on, an edit on a frame keys every layer there, one key deleted takes the frame from them all, and the timing chart and Path Timing graph have one “<group> together” row (none of its layers') whose timing edits and right-click menu reach them all; each layer still traces its own path. A filler edited since (its value, or the timing of the stretch into or out of it) becomes the layer's own. A layer that joins while it is on (moved in, made, pasted or duplicated inside, or a group moved in) takes the group's frames and gives the group its own, deleting nothing; one moved out lets go of the fillers it never edited, as if turned off, keeping its own keys (moved straight into another group that is on, it takes that one's frames). Turned off, the fillers never edited go, each layer moving between its own keys again (a whole drawing's keys on a frame go or stay together; those in its actions go too); the rest stay, and the Info editor says how many went and stayed. On again adds them again, and undo puts them back. Saved with the file, fillers marked. Python: layer.key_together; the motion path operators take drawing=1 with the group's layer=. | Toon Boom, Moho, Blender | v1 |
| **Breakdown keys** (Blender's key type BREAKDOWN): a key with its own look. Every key has a type, Key or Breakdown; a breakdown works as any key (its stretches timed on their own, by their own interpolation; the timing tools and the timeline treat it as a key), only drawn apart. Drawn as a small dark-blue circle in the timeline rows, the timing chart and on the motion path (light blue selected). Alt Shift's slide along the arc on a motion path makes a breakdown (Alt click adds a control point, below). The Key Type menu beside the timeline's interpolation sets new keys' type (no key selected) or the selected keys'; T menu > Key Type and the timing chart's right-click > Key Type convert keys. Paste Interpolation and Use for New Keys leave the type alone. Kept together across Key Whole Drawing and Key Together: a drawing's or group's keys on a frame are selected and set together. Frame 0 is never a breakdown. Saved with the file. Python: key dicts' "type", keyframe_set(type="KEYFRAME" or "BREAKDOWN"), anim.keys_interpolation_set(type=), anim.motion_path_key_interp(type=), anim.motion_path_key_add(type=, default BREAKDOWN); wm.motion_path_timing()'s stretches list their "breakdowns". | Blender, Moho, tweenMachine | v1 |
| **Time Together** (the timeline's chain toggle, off for new documents): changing the timing of keys changes it the same way on the linked channels. A channel is linked when it belongs to a selected layer (its move, turn, size, shear and opacity, its drawing's shape) or a selected bone (position, angle, scale, depth), has none of its own keys selected, and is keyed on both ends of the stretch edited: from the unmoved key before the selected keys to the unmoved key after (or the last moved key, where nothing comes after). A chain marks linked rows in the timeline. Moving or scaling keys (G, S, Move Keys, Scale Keys) takes the linked keys through the same stretch, so a key halfway along stays halfway; they are rounded to whole frames and kept a frame apart. A move that changes nothing (onto the setup frame, where keys never go) is refused, no undo step. Squeeze is the default: only the stretches next to the moved keys change. Ripple (Ctrl while moving, ripple=1) moves the keys after the last moved one along by as much. While keys move, the frame counts to the keys either side show on the timeline. Dragging between two selected keys slides them (slide=1), a frame short of the keys either side, so the stretch between keeps its length. Timing edits (Set Interpolation, the Key Type menu, Paste Interpolation, the timing brushes in the viewport or anim.motion_path_stroke, the Path Timing graph and timing chart, Even Spacing) give the edited key's whole timing to the linked key on its frame where the linked channel is keyed on the same keys either side and nowhere between, a straight linked stretch taking a Custom timing keeping its straight way (operators flagged OPTYPE_TIMING, OperatorType::timing saying which runs retime: not a shape brush's stroke or Reset Shape, which keep the timing; a viewport stroke through TimeTogetherEdit; time_together_timing_follow in anim/animation.cc). + and - in the timeline (anim.span_frame_insert, anim.span_frame_remove, anim.span_frames_add(amount=)) add or take a frame in the stretch under the playhead on the active layer's channels, the keys after going along, never below one frame, and the linked channels keyed on the same two frames go along. Match Timing (the key menu, anim.match_timing) keys the other selected layers' and bones' animated channels on the active layer's frames, stretched over each one's own first to last key: with as many keys as frames each keeps its pose, else each key goes to the nearest frame and the rest are keyed with what the channel held there. Alt (together=0) does just the one channel. Each edit is one undo step, and the Info editor says how many channels went along. Saved with the file. Python: oanim.data.document.time_together, oanim.wm.time_together_linked() (the marked channels as (layer id, path)). | Maya's Retime Tool, Cascadeur, animBot, Animation Retimer | v1 |
| Motion paths, as Blender's and Maya's motion trails. **Stage 1 (done), seeing them:** the Motion Paths button in the timeline's header turns them on, its own popover beside it holding their settings (between the onion skins' and auto keying's, which is last). They trace the selected bones' tips and heads (two paths, "arm tip" and "arm head", each with its own timing row and style; a head connected to its parent has none, riding on the parent's tip), the selected points (through their bones; a point with only a handle picked traces too), or with nothing picked the layer's origin, as the keys leave them (deforms aside). One dot per frame, coloured by Dot Colour: Timing (Current Stretch), the default, colours each dot by its time through its stretch between keys, blue at its start through teal and green to yellow at the key it arrives at (a slice of viridis), but only on the stretch being worked on (the one the frame shown is in, both either side on a key, and those holding selected dots), the rest light grey and a little see-through; Timing (All) colours every stretch; Speed by speed, red where fast to blue where still (against the path's own fastest frame); Path Colour the path's own (files from before read their Speed Colours switch as Timing or Path Colour). Choosing the Motion Path tool turns motion paths on if they are off. The line is the path's own shape where it comes from a position channel (`MotionPath::shape`, filled by `path_shape_fill` in `motion_path.cc`: each stretch's curve, through its control points, fitted onto the two dots of each step as drawn, so a moving, turning or flipped parent is followed): sliding dots or any timing change leaves it where it is, a crease at a control point is a sharp corner, and a dot backing up goes back along it. Elsewhere (a bone tip carried by its bone turning, a point bent by bones, a dot off its curve) it is a smooth curve through the dots (centripetal Catmull-Rom, `path_line_between` in `viewport.cc`): through every dot and never past one, a key's corner (Linear into Linear) a corner and a hold still. It is dashed only where it overshoots. Path lines take their colour as wires do: the first path is #4D9191, switching to #5CA8AE or #3A7070 only where it is hard to see; other paths keep their palette colours, and a path's set colour makes its own lighter and darker. A path handle is light violet (#C3A6FF; Preferences > Overlay Colours > Path Handle) with no outline, a lighter or darker violet only where hard to see, fading to orange at a tip being dragged; its line (2.5 px) is drawn over the path, the path's dots under it left out, so one lying along the path reads as a bar over it, and its tip, twice a dot's size, sits in a thin white ring, bigger and brighter under the mouse or while dragged. Outside the Motion Path tool paths are only looked at: their dots and keys all take the path's colour (lighter or darker where hard to see, as the line), keys still diamonds (breakdowns hollow) a touch lighter than the dots, with no outlines, the frame shown still ringed; nothing lights under the mouse, picks don't show, and Own Motion waits for the tool, so the real path shows. Back in the tool, the timing colours, white keys and picks return. Path handle tips are round (hollow while Auto). Keys as white diamonds (orange edged when selected), a ring on this frame; held frames merge into one dot with a count. Range: Keys Before and Keys After (1 and 1), counted on the keys of the traced thing and everything moving it, or All; shaded on the timeline. Step (dots every N frames), Lines, a colour per path, Relative To World, Camera or a Parent (the active layer and bone, so a hand can be watched against the chest). Key handles, control points and the brushes work on such a path where it is drawn, turned and scaled with what it is drawn against. Through control points the dots go as the stretch's speeds say, by length along the line through them. Undo leaves the paths shown, as it leaves the view. A dot in an overshoot (past its key and back) slides on along the line it goes there by, and sliding a dot leaves an overshoot elsewhere in the stretch as it is. G on the key of a bone held in a box (a controller's knob, a pupil in its eye) goes as far as the edge. Hovering a dot rings it and shows its frame number, with no ghost of the drawing there (it hid the paths). Pin keeps a path when it isn't selected; Snapshot keeps a faint copy to compare an edit against. Alt Space (Play Path Range) loops just the range from its start, Esc goes back, Space stops. Settings, pins and colours save with the file. **Stage 2 (done), the Motion Path tool** (General tools; shows the paths even with the popover's switch off): clicking and dragging as the Edit brush below says (it began as: a click selects a dot's key, Alt click goes to its frame, dragging a dot between keys adds a key there); a dot dragged is keyed on its own frame, the frame shown staying. Dragging a key moves it; either is keyed whatever auto-key says, and a bone's tip turns the bone (its head moves it; a tip whose bone has no angle keys, only position keys, goes as its head does, timed and moved by those). The Motion Path tool stays on as another kind of layer is made active (other tools go with their kind of layer). Ctrl puts it on the straight line between the keys either side; Shift between keys makes a breakdown, sliding along the arc from the key before to the key after (as tweenMachine); Esc or right-click cancels. The header sets the selected keys' interpolation (and an ease's strength), with a ball crossing a strip at it, dots showing its spacing frame by frame; hovering an option in the list moves the ball that way. Smooth Arc (`anim.motion_path_smooth_arc`, from Python only: no longer in the right-click menu) moves each key inside the range onto a smooth curve (a parabola over time) through up to two keys either side. **Stage 3 (done), path handles**: a selected key on a path traced by a position (a layer's origin, a bone's head, not a connected one, a point) shows two handles, in from the key before and out to the next, as a Bezier's, in the position keys' own space so they turn and scale with what carries them. They are Auto (hollow) until one is dragged, the path running as it always has (on a Smooth stretch the slope Smooth gives the key, a third of the way; on any other pointing a third of the way to the key at the other end, so the stretch stays straight); dragging one makes them Aligned (filled), the other turning to stay in line and keeping its length, and Alt breaks them (a square), each going its own way from then on. A handle is picked on its own (orange): clicking one lets go of the path keys, dots and other handles (Shift adds or takes out handles only, never anything else), and picking a key, a dot, a bone or a point, or clicking nothing, lets go of the handles; the bones and points traced stay picked, as they make the paths show. A picked handle keeps its key's handles showing, a drag still picks and moves it in one go, and with handles picked G R S move, turn and scale them alone (each about its key), V and the right-click menu set their keys' type, X puts them back to Auto (deleting no key) and the timeline's key header edits the stretch each shapes (an in handle the one its key ends, an out handle the next). Like the keys and dots picked on paths, picking a handle is not an undo step. **Control points** (as Toon Boom's): Alt click on a path between two position keys (a layer's origin, a bone's head, a point) adds one there, picked, an Alt drag moving it at once. It shapes the stretch's path only: no key, no frame: added, it lies on the path as it was, every dot staying put (refused where a dot has gone past its key, off the path); moved, the dots go the same share of the way along the new path each frame as before (eases, holds and Custom speeds kept). The path runs smoothly through the stretch's control points in order, leaving and reaching the keys along their handles. Convert to Control Point, in a position key's right-click menu (the timeline's, the timing chart's and graph's, and the path's with keys selected), makes a key with a key either side a control point where it was, with the handles it had: the path keeps its shape and every dot stays on its frame, the two stretches now one, Custom, timed as they went (eases, holds and steps on 2s kept; an overshoot past that key, as a Bezier ease's, now runs along the path instead of straight on); greyed, saying why, on an end key, the setup frame or a Key Whole Drawing point's key; a turn or size keyed there stays (anim.motion_path_key_to_control(frame=, layer=, bone=, point=; else the keys selected on the paths)). Convert to Key, in a control point's right-click menu (and with control points picked), is the way back: a key where it is, on the frame whose dot was nearest it, the curve cut there and the other dots staying (anim.motion_path_control_to_key(end=, index=, layer=, bone=, point=; else the control points picked)). Each is stored where it is (file format 6; older files, which kept it relative to the keys either side, are converted on opening), so moving, turning or scaling the keys leaves it, only the curve's shape changing toward it, as a vector point's neighbours stay when it moves (mirrored actions and action bone offsets don't move it). Drawn as small hollow squares (orange picked), never like keys or dots; picked as path handles are (Shift adds), or with keys: Shift click on a key keeps the control points picked and Shift click on a control point keeps the keys (a dot or a handle still lets them go), and L with the mouse over a path picks the keys either end of the stretch there and its control points, not its dots (on a key, the stretches either side), adding to what is picked; Shift L lets go of them; off a path L is the drawing's Select Linked (`path_stretch_select` in `src/ops/motion_path_ops.cc`; the path under the mouse is `WindowManager::path_under`, set as the paths draw, found even while a click or a move keeps the hover off, so L right after one still works). Dragged, moved, turned and scaled with G R S (picked with keys: all together about the middle of them all, the pivot drawn there, each moved once, `motion_path_picks_transform`), deleted with X (the path as it was without them; keys picked with them deleted too). Saved with the file (Key::path_controls). A key put inside a stretch shaped on the path (control points or Custom speeds) by I, Toggle Keyframe or an edit with Auto Keying goes in where its dot is first, the rest of the stretch keeping its way and timing (`key_put` in `src/anim/animation.cc`). A key added on a path between two (anim.motion_path_key_add) takes the path's way and speed there as its handles (Aligned), so adding it creases nothing; the stretch's control points stay where they are, each going to the side of the new key it is on, and deleting a key gives the stretch joining its neighbours the control points of both sides, where they were. Each control point has handles that work as a vector point's (Key::path_control_handles, PathControlHandles in src/anim/channel.hh): added, the curve's own there (so the path and its dots stay as they were; on a Smooth stretch, timed by its curve's own parameter, which the control point changes, the timing is kept as Custom speeds), its own from then on, stored where they are as the point is (X on a handle makes them worked out smooth from the points either side again, until one is moved); Aligned as they come, so dragging one turns the other to stay in line, keeping its length, and Alt moves just that one and unaligns the point; Align Handles and Unalign Handles in the right-click menu (a handle picked alone turns into line with the other, the point picked turns both halfway each); G R S move, turn and scale a picked handle about its point, and R S on control points turn and scale their handles with them (one alone: about it, as a vector point's; several about their middle; the mouse measured from there, where the pivot is drawn: `motion_path_handles_pivot`, oanim.wm.motion_path_handles_pivot); X on a handle makes them smooth again, X on the point deletes it. They look and show as a key's path handles, drawn by the same code: shown while the control point or one of its handles is picked or dragged, the violet line over the path (its dots under it left out), the tip in a thin white ring, hollow while worked out and filled once moved, orange fading to violet when picked or dragged, bigger and brighter under the mouse; the control point itself is a white square (orange picked). The sculpt brushes fit them. They shape the path only: the timing stays. Saved with the file. Python: key dicts' "path_controls", anim.motion_path_control_add(frame=), anim.motion_path_control_move(frame=, index=, x=, y=), anim.motion_path_handle_select(control=), anim.motion_path_handle_select(control=, control_handle=1|2), anim.motion_path_control_handle_move(frame=, index=, out=, x=, y=, alone=), anim.motion_path_controls_align(aligned=); key dicts' "path_control_handles" (in, out, set, aligned). A stretch with a dragged handle at either end runs along the Bezier: Smooth by the curve's own timing, any other mode by its progress along the curve's length, so Linear is an even speed and Back and Elastic overshoot along the end's direction; Step still holds. Auto Handles (the path's right-click menu, or V) puts the selected keys' handles back. Saved with the keys (path_handles, path_in, path_out); Python: a position key's keyframes() dict has them, keyframe_set takes them, anim.motion_path_handle(frame, side=IN/OUT, x, y, broken) and anim.motion_path_handles_auto(). Mirroring an action bone action (Symmetrize) mirrors its keys' handles with them. A handle shows only where the path draws the stretch it shapes: none past the path's ends (as when the path ends on the frame shown). A handle with no length, of any type (Smooth flattens a key where the path turns back both across and up or down, as a bounce's top corner; a key frozen sharp keeps them so, Broken) still shows, short (30 px) along the way the path runs from the key, so it can be grabbed; showing it changes nothing. Stage 4 (done), path brushes: while the Motion Path tool is the tool a brush shelf runs along the bottom of the viewport, as Sculpt's and Draw's, with tabs All and Shape; a path's shape (where it goes) and its speed (how fast it goes along) are brushed apart, so a speed brush never bends the path and a shape brush never changes its timing. Edit is the tool as stages 2 and 3 have it; every other brush has its own radius (F, in screen pixels) and strength (Shift F) with a smooth falloff, works on the paths shown, keys whatever auto-key says, and is one undo step a stroke. Stage 5 (done), shape brushes: Grab, Smooth and Surface Smooth are the Sculpt tool's own, on the path as a line (`motion_path_sculpt_begin` and `motion_path_sculpt_dab` in `motion_path.cc`): when a stroke starts each path with position keys of its own is made into a line in a drawing of its own, the path as shown (from the key it starts on to the one it ends on, not the keys beyond: the line's ends are the path's, so Smooth and Surface Smooth keep them where they are, as a drawn curve's; an end key whose handles go in line turns its handle off the path with the one the brush shaped, and a stretch off the path is retimed only when that reshapes it), its keys and control points the line's points and the handles the path runs along its handles, and the Sculpt tool's stroke runs on it (sampled densely, each point anchored to its sample, the touched segments' handles fitted to the samples, a smooth point's handles kept in line); each dab gives the path back: keys where their points went, their handles as fitted (an Auto key becoming Aligned), control points where they went. Grab takes the samples under the brush when the stroke starts and drags them with the mouse; Smooth is Grease Pencil's Smooth on the resampled line; Surface Smooth takes out lumps up to its Lump Sensitivity while the arcs stay; with it picked a pale blue ghost shows the fit it moves the paths toward, as the Sculpt tool's (the stroke's own while one goes on, then kept; else the fit a stroke would start with now, fitted again when the paths, the frame or Lump Sensitivity change; not with the overlays off; motion_path_surface_fit, oanim.wm.motion_path_surface_ghost). Each has the Sculpt tool's radius, strength and falloff (Smooth, Sphere, Constant; brush_falloff= and sensitivity= on `anim.motion_path_brush_set` and `anim.motion_path_stroke`). No key is added or removed, the setup key (frame 0) stays where it is (its point pinned) though its out handle, how the path leaves the setup, is reshaped like any key's, keys off the paths shown stay, and each stretch reshaped keeps its timing: it becomes Custom with the speeds it had by length along it, so its dots stay at the same share of the way (a Noisy one stays Noisy, keeping its jitter). A bone turning its tip (no position keys) is still moved by its keys, as before; a target its position keys don't move (a controller's tip) is left out, as dragging its handles is refused. Stage 6 (done), speed brushes: speed is painted on the dots between keys, where how far apart they are is how fast it goes. Painting makes the stretch's timing Custom (a new interpolation mode, on the key ending the stretch as every mode is): each frame's dot as far along as its timing curve says (see Timing as a curve), the dots sliding along the same path; a stretch keeps its frames, so each key still arrives on its own frame and painting one part faster slows the rest of the stretch to make up for it (the frames not under the brush take it up first). Custom starts from how the stretch moved before, so nothing jumps; it is painted on the channel that moves the dot (a bone's angle for its tip, the position for a head, a point or a layer's origin). Blend Speed evens out the spacing; Ctrl sharpens it (slow parts slower, fast parts faster), the brush circle turning red while it does. (Paint Speed, Faster and Slower were tried and taken out: Blend Speed and the timing chart do it better.) The shelf's Timing tab holds the timing brushes, which never move a key or change the path's shape: Blend Speed; Pinch, which crowds the dots under the circle together so the move slows there (Ctrl spreads them: faster); and Slide, which drags the dots inside the circle along the path, the dot nearest the press going with the mouse and the ones nearer the circle's edge going less (the dots stay in order between their keys). Shape holds Grab and Smooth. Each brush keeps its own radius and strength. Shift swaps in a brush while held, as Sculpt's Shift is Smooth: with Grab, Smooth; with Pinch or Slide, Blend Speed; with Edit, a Shift drag paints Blend Speed (Ctrl Shift sharpens) while a Shift click on a dot or key without moving still adds it to the selection, and Alt Shift is still the breakdown; the circle names the Shift brush, which keeps its own radius and strength (with Edit in hand, F and Shift F size Blend Speed's). Retiming keys (a stretch growing longer) is not done: keys stay on their frames. Python: anim.motion_path_brush_set(brush=, radius=, strength=, chart_size=, graph_size=), oanim.wm.path_brush_settings(), anim.motion_path_stroke(brush=, points="x,y;x,y", radius= in document units, strength=, invert=, shift=) for a stroke; a Custom key's keyframes() dict has its speeds. The Edit brush (done; Edit Speed, a brush of its own at first, folded into it): a click selects a key or a dot between keys, Shift adds it or takes it out, a double click goes to its frame, a click on nothing lets go; selected keys show their path handles, selected dots an orange ring and their speed (x1.60). Key selection is the timeline's; the dots selected are kept by the tool (oanim.wm.motion_path_dots()). Dragging a key moves it; dragging a handle bends the path (Alt breaks the pair); dragging a dot between keys slides it along its path to the place nearest the mouse, never off it, the keys staying on their frames (its stretch becomes Custom). With proportional editing (O, Blender's, as for points; Shift O the falloff, from the Motion Paths popover too) the dots within its Influence (frames either side of the dragged dot, in the timeline's Motion Paths popover; the wheel or Page Up / Page Down while dragging) slide too by the falloff, tinted by how much; the reach stops at the keys either side, keys never move, and dots never pass each other or a key. Alt click on the path adds a key there, where it is (anim.motion_path_key_add), every dot staying where it was, those beside the stretch too (the keys either side keep their handles, shortened to the halves they now have; the new key arrives as the stretch did, Linear staying Linear, plays on as the key before did, on 2s or 3s too, and inside that key's hold holds for the rest of it; on 2s or 3s a held frame keys the frame whose pose it holds, none when that is the key before; with Key Whole Drawing every channel of the drawing is keyed in place on its own stretch, so no other point moves; the curve is split at the dot and its timing shared between the two halves, right up to its ends; on a dot in an overshoot, past its key or back before its start, the stretch into the new key is the old curve, its end a control point, then straight on to the key, and the one out of it straight back); Alt drag adds it and moves it, Ctrl putting it on the straight line between the keys either side; Alt Shift drag makes a breakdown along the arc. With the Edit brush a plain click picks a bone (of the active bone layer and its family) or a point (of the drawings being edited), as the Select tool's click does, and Shift click adds it, so its path shows. A path handle, key or dot wins over a bone under it, the dot of the frame shown too, though it sits on the bone or point it traces (that is picked already, as its path shows; a click there picking the bone instead made the key there hard to pick); a click on empty space clears only the path's keys and dots, the picked bones and points staying; so does Alt A while anything is picked on the paths (keys, dots, handles, control points; `anim.motion_path_deselect_all`), with nothing picked there it deselects the bones and points as ever. Off the paths the drawing is edited as with the Select tool: a point, a shown handle or (in Edges and Strokes modes) a line is clicked (Shift adds, Ctrl takes away) or dragged (a handle at once, a point once the mouse moves), a drag from empty space is a box, and a right click with points or handles picked (no path keys or dots) opens the point menu. Alt keeps its meanings (Alt click adds a key, Alt Shift a breakdown). While anything moves (G, R, S, a drag, a brush stroke) no path dot is hovered: no highlight and no ring or number, which wait after the move until the mouse moves on. With the other brushes, Alt click picks a bone or point instead and Alt Shift click adds it; its own spot wins over a path dot drawn on it, a path handle or control point under the mouse wins over it (so Alt drags a handle alone, still picked), and Alt elsewhere on the path keeps its meaning. A path starts at the scene's Start (frame 0 is the setup pose, not part of the shot): with a key on Start it begins there, with none it reaches back to the last key before Start (frame 0's when there is none), the part before Start drawn faded, so the stretch the shot opens in can still be shaped. It ends the same way at the scene's End: with a key on End there, with none on to the first key after End (that key fully editable, only drawn faded), the part past End faded; with no key after End, at End. Frame 0's key, the setup pose, can be picked but never moves (its diamond's centre hollow), is never deleted and is never retimed; picked, it shows only an out handle (Auto at first), how the path leaves the setup. Each action has its own frame 0 handle, and so does the mainline: kept while the action is put away (`Channel::action_setup`), saved, and carried along when the action is renamed or mirrored. Own Motion (the tool's header, on by default, saved): in the Motion Path tool every path, picked or not, shows only its own motion (one that turned real as something on it was picked or let go confused; outside the tool paths are only looked at, the real ones), whatever else moves it (the bones a point hangs from, parent bones, its layer and the groups above) held as on the frame shown, so it still runs through where the thing is now and follows as you scrub; nothing else is drawn with it (a faint ghost of the real path confused more than it told: Own Motion off shows the real path, and Relative To only shows with it off), and dots, keys and handles dragged land where they are drawn, only the target's own keys changing. Only the paths are held: the drawing plays as ever (`motion_path_own_motion`, `path_evaluate` in `motion_path.cc`; `MotionPathOnFrame` keeps the frame shown). A bone's own constraints are left out of its own motion too (a pupil's Look At or Limit to Shape): the line, dots, handles and control points follow the curve its keys and control points shape, and a key dragged goes where it is put, past where the constraint would stop it (`own_constraints_off`; Own Motion off shows where the constraint really puts it). Where the dots, key handles and control points are is found by working whole frames out on copies of the document (places), shared out over the CPU's cores, one thread a core (each place streams the whole document through memory: on six cores about twice as fast as one; one at a time where bones have dynamics). A control point is two places and its handles' tips two more, worked out only for a picked or dragged one, and by Move, G, R and S only where they move apart from it (`held_places`, `places_side_by_side`; oanim.wm.motion_path_places counts them). On the eye rig a control point's drag step takes 36 ms instead of 95, the paths and key handles 47 instead of 62. With keys selected, as Blender's transform (numbers typed, Ctrl snapping, Shift finer, click or Enter keeps it, Esc or a right click cancels): one key, G moves it, R turns its handles about it and S scales them; several keys, G R S move, turn and scale the keys about their centre, their handles going with them (anim.motion_path_keys_transform(kind=G/R/S, x=, y=, angle=, factor=)). V: a menu of handle types, Auto (as before they were dragged), Aligned (in line, each keeping its length) and Broken (anim.motion_path_handles_type(mode=)); X or Delete deletes the selected keys, every key of its own on their frames, a turn's as much as a move's, as the diamond's Delete Key does (anim.motion_path_keys_delete). Alt S scales the timing around each dot and key selected: as S in Blender, the mouse's distance from the selection (a dashed line runs from it to the mouse) against its distance when pressed: further spreads the dots out (faster), nearer brings them closer (slower), the dot staying; on a key, the dots either side come in toward it, easing into and out of the pose. The dots within the Influence scale by the falloff, always (O is left as it is; Python's proportional=0 scales them all alike); keys stay and dots never cross; numbers typed, Ctrl snaps to 0.1, Shift finer, the wheel sizes the reach; a dashed line with the factor. Even Spacing (in the path's right-click menu) makes the selected dots' stretches go at an even speed. The right-click menu: on a path dot between keys or on a control point, that stretch's Reset Stretch (a straight line at an even speed, its control points gone), Reset Shape (straight, each dot at the same share of the way as before: the timing kept, a Smooth stretch, timed by its curve, made Custom with the speeds it had) and Reset Timing (an even speed along the shape it has; the only one on a drawing's path), `anim.motion_path_stretch_reset` (frame=, what=BOTH, SHAPE or TIMING), the keys either side staying where they are and the stretches beside them keeping their shape (a key there turns a corner, Broken, only where its sides no longer line up); with keys selected Align Handles, Auto Handles, Break Handles, Convert to Control Point and Delete Keys; with dots selected Even Spacing; and Go to Frame; each one undo step. The tool's bar holds only Own Motion, Edit Layer (and with a brush other than Edit, its Radius and Strength): interpolation is the timeline's key header, and proportional editing (on/off, falloff, Influence) is in the timeline's Motion Paths popover. With the tool (whether the Motion Paths overlay is on or off; never without the tool) there is one selected stretch, shared by the timeline's key header, its rows, the timing chart and the Path Timing tab, and shown teal (red stays the selected objects'): the stretch the picks are in (a picked dot's; a picked key's on the side the playhead is on, with the playhead on the key the one arriving at it, a path's first and last keys having one side; a picked path handle's), else, with nothing picked, the stretch under the playhead of the path being edited (the one last picked on, else the first traced). The header turns teal and names it beside it ("Ball 1  10-20", or "2 paths  10-20" when picks on several paths share its frames) and edits its end key, selected only while the header acts, never into an undo step (a key picked with the playhead after it is left out meanwhile and picked again after). It is shaded teal only on the row of the channel moving the picked dot (a bone's head its Transform row, a turning tip its Angle row; folded away, the row it is folded into; never the Summary), and in its timing chart row. Picks in different stretches make no teal: the header is grey, "2 stretches", and edits them all; other keys selected as well make it red, editing them with the picks' stretches. Alt click in a timeline row picks a stretch: with the tool, the row's dots on the frame clicked (a bone's row its tip's and head's, a layer's or the Summary every path shown; on a key, the key; Alt Shift adds; a row with no path traced does nothing; two quick Alt clicks are two picks, never the double-click that keys a row), the frame going there, not an undo step; without it, the key ending the stretch there (`anim.timeline_stretch_pick`; Blender's Alt click selects a column instead). Edit Layer (the bar's checkbox, off by default, kept in the tool settings; `ToolSettings::path_edit_layer`, edit_layer= on `anim.motion_path_brush_set`) edits the layer itself: only its origin's path shows (position only), whatever bones or points are picked; clicks never pick bones or points and they aren't drawn; the Transform Layer box goes round the layer, taking the presses the paths leave, and its moves, turns and scales are keyed on the frame shown whatever Auto Keying says (`layer_gizmo_finish`); the teal is on the layer's transform rows; G R S and Snap work on the layer too, as the Transform Layer tool's, never the points picked. Off, the tool edits bones and points, and the teal never falls on the layer's own position. Python: anim.motion_path_select(frame=, extend=, go=, none=), anim.motion_path_slide(frame=, x=, y=, proportional=, size=, falloff=), anim.motion_path_even_spacing(frame=, else the dots selected), anim.motion_path_scale_spacing(frame=, else around the selection, factor=, proportional=, size=, falloff=). Timing in the timeline (done): while the Motion Path tool is the tool, a timing chart runs along the bottom of the timeline, a row for each thing traced (up to four): each stretch a line from key to key (white diamonds); a row and a path have only their own keys (a point's moves; a bone's moves, turns and sizes, and its knob's keys when a controller plays it; a layer's turns, moves and sizes), since a bone moving a point or a parent bone or group above has its own row to retime it in, and a point only a bone moves has no row with a tick on each frame placed by how far along its path that frame has got, as an animator's timing chart, coloured as the dots. With any brush (the brush is what a drag on the path does in the viewport; the chart is a place of its own) a tick is a dot: a click selects it (Ctrl adds, a double click goes to its frame), a drag left or right slides it along the path, Esc or a right click cancels. Proportional editing is always on in the chart and the graph (O doesn't apply there), each with its own size in frames (3 at first, 0 the dot alone): a soft band of that many frames either side of the dot under the mouse (an outline circle in the graph), its number beside it, the wheel resizing it while dragging (the dots following at once), the dots it pulls ringed faintly; the falloff is the tool's. The keys' diamonds in the chart and graph are keys too: a click selects one as clicking it on the path does (Ctrl adds or takes it out), lit orange, so the timeline's X deletes it; a right click opens its menu, Interpolation (the timeline's modes and Strength, the key's own ticked), Convert to Control Point and Delete Key, for the key behind it: the path's own key there (a whole drawing's: every channel's), else its layer's or a group's above that moves it there (turn, move or size); one undo step each (anim.motion_path_key_interp(frame=, interp=, ease=, ... as anim.keys_interpolation_set takes them), anim.motion_path_key_delete(frame=), with layer= and bone=, point= or drawing=1). A Shift click or drag blends the timing there as a brush, as Blender's Smooth Vertices on the way along, a blend on each frame the mouse reaches (every frame between too, so speed doesn't matter; gone over again, again; the graph's stays on its stretch): each dot within Radius frames (fading to its edge) moves Smoothing of the way to the midpoint of its neighbours, Repeat times, the keys staying; each dab blends again by as much, and a stroke is one undo step. Its settings (Smoothing 0.5, Repeat 1, Radius 3 frames, its own reach, not the drag's) are in the timeline's Adjust Last Operation panel, which runs the last stroke again, and are kept for the next; while Shift is held a blue band (a circle in the graph) shows the Radius, and the wheel sizes it, a frame a notch, mid-stroke too (anim.motion_path_timing_blend(frame=, frames="15,16,17", smoothing=, repeat=, radius=)). Alt S over the timeline scales the timing around the ticks and keys picked, as Alt S on the path: the mouse's distance sideways from them (an orange line down the chart, a dashed line out to the mouse) against its distance when pressed, further spreading the ticks out (faster), nearer bringing them in (slower); the chart's reach (the wheel sizes it), numbers typed, Ctrl snaps to 0.1, Shift finer; a click or Enter keeps it, one undo step, Esc or a right click cancels (`anim.timeline_scale_spacing_modal`, then anim.motion_path_scale_spacing). Over the chart, when nothing is dragged, the wheel zooms the timeline; hovering a tick rings its dot in the viewport, and the other way round. The Path Timing editor (an editor of its own, next to the Timeline in an area's editor menu, the graph taking the area's height but never taller than it is wide, so a tall area leaves room under its controls rather than a long box; the timeline has no sidebar) shows one stretch at a time, the selected stretch (with picks in different stretches the last picked, "1 of 2", its arrows stepping through them and keeping the picks; with one stretch picked its arrows pick the stretch beside, the dot in its middle or its end key, the frame going there; with nothing picked the one the frame shown is in, or one stepped to with its arrows until the frame changes); a graph of its timing curve: how far along (0 to 1 along the path) against its frames, the even speed a dashed diagonal, each frame's dot small and faint on the curve, the neighbouring stretches' dots grey and faint at its edges. Under the graph, Edit and Sculpt, then a thin line and that mode's settings; an editor too short for them and the graph at its smallest scrolls (the wheel over the graph still zooms it). Edit shows the curve's points (its ends are the keys' diamonds) with handles that work as a drawing's: an aligned point's other handle turns to stay in line, Alt drag moves one alone, which breaks the point (and Alt drag on the curve away from any handle puts a point there and drags it, one undo step), and a handle reaches no further across than its segment, so the curve only goes forward in time; a click picks a point or handle and Shift click adds or takes one out, a drag from empty space draws a box (Shift adds, Ctrl takes away), A picks all and Alt A none, the last clicked being the active one, all let go if the stretch's end key moves; a point drags anywhere between its neighbours in time, and a drag on one of several picked moves them all (a click on it without moving picks it alone); Ctrl click adds one where clicked, picked alone; G moves the picks (X keeps them to time, Y to the way along, numbers typed in frames and %, Ctrl snaps to whole frames and 5%, Enter or a click keeps it, Esc or right click cancels), stopping short of the next point not picked, the ends never moving, a handle picked alone moving with an aligned point's other kept in line, both of a point's picked moving together; R turns each picked point's handles about it as the graph shows them; S scales one point's handles, or several points' places about their middle with their handles (S X, S Y), never past a point not picked; V and the right click menu's Handles make a picked point's handles Aligned (in line along the average of their two directions, each keeping its length), Broken or Flat (level), pressing one again changing nothing; each is one undo step; X or Delete over the graph deletes the picked points (never an end), the right click menu's Decimate takes out the points the curve changes least without (the handles either side fitted again, pieces it doesn't merge left as they were) until its Ratio of those between the ends is left, the Ratio (half the first time, then the last used) in the Adjust Last Operation panel in the editor's bottom-left corner, and the Point row has the active point's frame and % along. Sculpt has its Brush, Grab or Smooth (a small pair of their own under the label), Radius in frames, Strength, Lock X (off, Grab moves the curve in time too, as Blender's and Maya's do, by how near each part was to where the brush was pressed: the part it was nearest goes with the mouse, behind it time stretches within the circle, and ahead it squeezes within the circle while that holds the move, reaching further ahead (never behind, never past the key) only as a longer drag needs, smoothly, so time never runs backward and nothing has a corner; on, only up and down) and beside it Dyntopo (as a drawing's sculpt, the brushes move fine samples of the curve and the curve is fitted back to them, the pieces a stroke didn't move left exactly as they were; off to start with: each point goes where its sample went and the pieces moved have their handles fitted again, a smooth point's in line (its handle on a piece the stroke didn't move turning to stay so, keeping its length, so that piece bends a little), a piece whose samples only go one way going that way too, none added or taken out, so pressed anywhere on a long piece it still follows the brush; on, those pieces get a point every frame, then the points it made or moved that the curve keeps its shape without go, held to two pixels on a long path, a smooth bend one piece); the brush is a circle with a ring where its falloff is half, Shift swaps the brush while held, F over the graph sizes it (click to keep, Esc to go back), and a stroke shows live and is one undo step (one that moves nothing is none). Each mode's keys and settings are in tooltips, not text in the panel. Its view zooms and pans as the viewport's: the wheel zooms around the mouse when nothing is dragged, a middle drag pans, Ctrl middle drag stretches the axes; Home fits it, and so does showing another stretch (the whole stretch, overshoot too, with a margin), while edits on the same stretch never move it. The right click's menu has Even, Ease In, Ease Out and Ease In-Out for the whole stretch (Custom, the keys staying) and Even Spacing (on a key, the key's menu). Without the Motion Path tool the editor asks for it. Timing as a curve (done): a Custom stretch's timing is kept as a curve of how far along against the share of its time (Key::timing, anim/timing_curve.hh), not a speed for each frame: whatever makes it (a speed brush, a dot slid in the chart or the viewport, a shape, a sculpt stroke) gives each frame's progress, and the curve keeps as few points as put every frame within 0.0002 of the way of it (a smooth timing a handful, a shape just its two ends, noise one a frame), so files stay small; points placed in Edit stay as placed, and so does the curve when a shape brush reshapes the path (it goes by share of the way, whatever the way) or an edit changes nothing (Alt S by 1, a dot slid to where it is). A stretch not yet Custom is measured by how far along its path each frame is (not by straight steps, which cut a turn's corner), a frame held still measuring still, so the chart says what plays and making it Custom moves no dot. The curve stretches with the stretch when its keys move. Files store it (format 7); an older file's speeds open as the curve they make. Overshoot (done): in the timing chart a dot drags past its key (up to twice the way) and comes back to it, or back before its start (anticipation), and the graph's curve can go there too; a Custom stretch's curve goes over 1 or under 0, and the dot goes on past the key along the way the path arrives (back from the start along the way it leaves), as Back and Elastic do. The graph keeps room above the key and below the start, shaded; the chart puts those ticks on a raised lane with a line back to their key; the viewport draws the path dashed there. The viewport's own drag keeps dots in order between the keys; Alt S keeps an overshoot; anim.motion_path_dot_speed can go under 0. Python: oanim.wm.motion_path_timing() (each traced thing's stretches: start, end, progress; on 2s or 3s its interval and played, what each frame plays: the chart has ticks only for frames that play a pose of their own, and sliding a held frame's dot slides the frame whose pose it holds), anim.motion_path_slide(progress=), anim.motion_path_stretch_shape(shape=, frame=), anim.motion_path_dot_speed(frame=, speed=), anim.motion_path_timing_step(step=), oanim.wm.motion_path_timing_panel(), oanim.wm.editor_types(); the graph's anim.timing_point_move(index=, x=, y=), anim.timing_handle_move(index=, side=IN/OUT, x=, y=, free=), anim.timing_point_add(x=, y=), anim.timing_point_delete(index=), anim.timing_sculpt(brush=GRAB/SMOOTH, path="frame,along;...", radius=, strength=, aspect=, lock_x=) and anim.timing_graph_set(mode=EDIT/SCULPT, brush=, radius=, strength=, lock_x=); a key's "timing" (its curve's points) and "speeds" (each frame's, from the curve) in keyframes(), and keyframe_set(timing=). | Blender, Maya | v1 |
| Sound in the timeline: a waveform on each clip with sound, Scrubbing, Play Audio and Sync to Audio (see Media layers) | Both | v1 |
| Playback controls: loop, preview range, and Blender's Playback popover (from the timeline header's Playback menu, opening above it): Sync (Play Every Frame shows every frame even when that plays slower; Frame Dropping, the default, skips frames to keep the true speed; Sync to Audio follows the sound being heard), Audio (Scrubbing, Play Audio), Playback (Loop Playback, Stepped Preview) and Keying (Auto Keying); saved with the project (Python: document.sync_mode NONE / FRAME_DROP / AUDIO_SYNC, play_audio, use_audio_scrub) | Blender | v1 |

**Modifiers**

| Feature | From | Tier |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Modifier stack on vector layers, applied non-destructively | Blender | v1 |
| Mirror modifier for drawings; bone layers get Blender's Symmetrize instead (Armature menu and the Bones panel), not a mirror modifier | Blender | v1 |
| Noise, smooth, offset, thickness and simplify modifiers | Blender | Later |
| Array, build, dash, outline and envelope modifiers | Blender | Later |

**Armature modifier.** The bones moving a drawing are a modifier too: every layer inside a bone layer has one Armature modifier, added and removed with the bone layer and never deleted by hand. Its place in the stack decides what the bones move: a Mirror modifier above it mirrors the setup drawing, so each half follows its own side's bones (a point bound or weighted to `arm_L` mirrors to `arm_R`); below it, the posed drawing is mirrored. The stack runs top to bottom, each modifier working on what the ones above it made, and Add Modifier puts a new one at the bottom, as Blender does (drag it above the Armature modifier to work on the setup drawing).

**Boolean modifier.** Joins a layer's shapes and other layers' into one outline, as Moho's Liquid Shapes join shapes, but across layers, so each part keeps its own bones and draw order. Add it from Add Modifier > Boolean on the layer that owns the look. Its settings: **Shapes** (which of the layer's own shapes merge: All shapes, every one that goes all the way round, or the ones ticked); **Layers** (the layers merged in, a row each: the layer, a dropdown as Parent Bone's with a search field on top (focused as it opens; any part of the name, any case; Enter takes the first left), picking another layer to merge in its place; its shapes, a dropdown of checkboxes with its own search; an eyedropper; and x to stop merging it; then an Add Layer... row, the same dropdown and eyedropper. The eyedropper takes a shape clicked in the viewport and its layer together: on Add Layer... it adds them, on a layer's row it puts them in that layer's place, and the shape clicked is what it takes: added to a layer's picks, or picked alone on a layer taking all its shapes. While it's on, the cue at the mouse names what a click would take, "Layer | Shape" (Ear_L | S7), as every shape eyedropper does. It looks past the merging layer itself, which may be drawn over what it merges, and a shape not going all the way round can't merge, so it waits for another click); **Operation** (Union: one outline round them all; Subtract: the others cut away, the outline running on along the cut; Intersect: only where they overlap); **Smoothing** (a radius in the layer's units, keyable and drivable: it rounds the corners where the outlines meet, and only there, so the shapes' own drawn corners stay sharp; 0 keeps the joins sharp); **Corners Only** (with Union: the layer's shapes just grow the rounded corners where they meet the others, taking nothing from them); and **Apply** (the header menu's Apply, or Ctrl A over it; on Setup, frame 0: the merged outline becomes the layer's drawing, the shapes it took leave their layers, and the modifier goes). The result takes the first own shape's place in the layer and its look (fill, line, brush and material); the line keeps the width it had where each part came from, and a brushed line lays its stamps along the merged outline as it is. The other layers are merged as they're posed (their bones and modifiers first), and a Boolean runs at its place in the stack, as every modifier does: a Bevel below it bevels the joined outline, above it only the layer's own shapes. **What it takes:** what its own shape draws. A layer that draws fill and line takes the merged shapes whole, so they stop drawing on their own layers and nothing is drawn twice; an outline-only layer takes just their lines, their fills still drawing on their own layers; a fill-only one just their fills. The rest of a merged-in layer (an ear's inner line, say) still draws on that layer, at its own place in the stack, and clicking a merged part still picks its own layer. So a rig that draws its lines on their own layers (a head's fill and a Head_Outline copy) gets an ear joined with a Boolean on the outline layer (Ear_L's base shape, with Smoothing) and a Corners Only Boolean on the fill layer with the same Smoothing, so the rounded corners are filled too. Hidden, a layer isn't joined (Union); as a cutter (Subtract, Intersect) it cuts hidden or not, as a mask shape does, and isn't drawn. Two layers merged together don't mask each other: their mask steps naming each other (a Join Lines pair) are left out, so lines aren't cut where the outline now runs. Two layers merging each other stop there. Merged geometry is worked out with Clipper2 (polygon booleans and offsets: grown then shrunk by the radius near where the outlines cross, which rounds those corners) and turned back into curves, smooth where they bend gently and sharp where they turn hard. Python: oanim.ops.object.modifier_add(type="BOOLEAN", targets="Ear_L"), object.modifier_boolean_set(index=, targets=, operation=, smoothing=, corners_only=, shapes=, target=, target_shapes=, and the eyedropper's pick= with pick_shape= and replace=), object.modifier_apply(index=); layer.modifiers gives a Boolean's targets, target_shapes, shapes, operation, smoothing and corners_only; layer.evaluated() says of each shape what another layer's Boolean draws of it ("ALL", "FILL", "LINE"). Drivers and keys: modifiers[i].smoothing. It was called Merge at first: files saying so open with a Boolean (a default name "Merge" becomes "Boolean").

**Bevel modifier.** Cuts a drawing's corners off and rounds them, Blender's Bevel modifier in Vertices mode, for curves. Add it from Add Modifier > Bevel; it goes at the bottom of the stack, so it bevels the posed drawing and a corner keeps its size however it's bent (dragged above the Armature modifier, the bones bend the bevelled drawing).
